# Volume 05 continuity — Batch 0001, Chapters 201 to 210

**The eighteenth year after the Long Fracture, the first to the eleventh of the fourth month. The month turned once, in Chapter 201, and it was said once. Volume 04 is closed at Chapter 200 and this section is additive; nothing in it corrects a Volume 04 record except where a figure is named, and where a figure is named the chapter is the authority.**

## The world in this block

- **A Registry.** A body that holds records of holdings. **It does not keep a book a stranger can walk up to, and the block's first sentence about it, said by a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing in a lane on the second of April, is that this district has the one place in this basin that does.** It prints a house and it prints a holder. **An office may print a house and may not print a person, and a name a body has printed is not a person who agreed to be there — that is the panel at Chapter 207 and it is the same rule the registrar of this district's records office has said out loud four times in five months, and the Registry's man said on the fourth of April that it is the same rule the district has.** Two bodies, one rule, and a rule two bodies hold is two rules that happen to look the same.
- **The Registry man.** About forty-four, a lease clerk, thin, carrying a flat case the size of a hymn book. Nine days in the district, from the second to the eleventh of April, **sleeping five nights on a bench outside the door with the fanlight over it that nobody gave him.** He was never asked to leave and never asked to stay. **He refused the place three times, on the second, the third and the seventh, and never once refused outright: *I am not going to say it today and I am not refusing to say it and both of those are true.*** **He never answered the third of the six things, which is the year.** He answered the fourth, the term. He did not answer the fifth, the toll. He answered the sixth, who he came for: *I have not come for anybody. There is a man in this basin whose name I have and I did not get it here.* **He said on the seventh, out loud and unasked, that in five days in this district nobody once asked him what he came for, and that what he came for was not a bearer.** He said his body enters a no and dates it and that the asking is the answer. **He said on the seventh that a keeper is a person who is on a hill and is not on a page, and that there are four generations of them where his lease is, and that none of them has ever been on a page of his — and he gave no date, no year and no place, and a clerk of nineteen years entered the four generations as the first stranger's figure anybody in this yard has entered.**
- **The lease.** **A lease is renewed. The term ended on the last day of the second month and the renewal is dated the first of the fourth month. The use is a road. The toll is paid at the ninth place. The holder is the Registrar-General, Cael Orin. The place is named on the lease; this is not the lease.** **A term, a renewal, a date that is late and a date that is due are four different things and there is no column in this district for any of the four.** The bearer **does not have to be from this district**, which is on the second line and is a fact about the page and not a rule. **The lease has to be answered at the place on the last day of the fourth month, and the place has nobody at it but him.** The Registry man is not a clerk of a house and is not in any of the four refusal lists.
- **The schedule of nine rows.** A hand copy exists and the original is in the stranger's case. **It has nine rows and a column on the left with a number in it and a column on the right headed OPEN. Eight of the nine carry a day in that column. The ninth carries a word, and the word is IT IS NOT A ROAD.** **The eight days, read out on the ninth of April and entered in a book on the tenth, in this order: the first of the fourth month, the second, the third, the fourth, the sixth, the eighth, the tenth, the eleventh.** **Eight days inside eleven days, one a night, and the fifth, seventh and ninth carry nothing. The sheet does not say whether a day under that heading is a day the thing was open or a day it is to be open, and this district does not settle it; four of the eight had already happened when the sheet was read on the fifth of April and four had not.** A word in a column headed OPEN is a finding and not a blank. **A copy a man makes in fifty minutes and gives to a clerk is a copy and is not a fourth document nobody owns.**
- **Locks and gates.** **The people who keep them call them locks. The Registry calls them gates. The word on the Registry's own sheet is gates.** This district used the Registry's word for five days without knowing it was borrowed. **On the ninth of April a man of about thirty at the counter, carrying four sheets for a house thirty-four miles east, gave the district the house's word in fifty-three words: his house has called them locks since before anybody living was born, and has a word for the man who keeps one and a word for the night he turns it.** A word is not a figure, is not a schedule, is not a column and is not a document. **Nobody in this district has seen a keeper and no keeper has ever been on a page of anybody's, and four generations of them have never been on one.**
- **The road.** **There is one road in this district that climbs. It goes out past the loams and up past the top field of a farm four miles out, and the last of it is two hundred yards of grade that has been soft since a water came down a channel on the twenty-nighth of the third month.** The man of about thirty-one has tried to pull a dray up it once and failed. **The top field is the fourth holding on the delivery sheet at the second reservoir, and the fourth holding has not taken a drop since the first of the second month, and the man of about thirty-four who digs loam opened a sluice for it on the night of the thirteenth of the third month.** **None of that is joined to the road a route keeper says somebody opened on the night of the twenty-second of the second month nine miles away, and the joining was refused on the page twice.**

## The instruments, and the one that failed

- **The yard of Lot Seventeen is the district's instrument and this block is the first one in which it is used on a person who is not in it.** A yard is a room. A room is not a party and was refused entry as a seventh instrument in Chapter 204. **On the ninth of April about nineteen people in a yard decided what happened to a piece of wood on a hill, and one of them was a stranger from a body that prints houses and prints no people.**
- **The registrar's office is a room and is the only instrument in this district that holds a record.** **On the tenth of April eight printed days went into a book in that office and she said in sixty-one words why: a day a stranger has printed is a day somebody is going to be told about, and a day in a book is a day a person can be asked about, and this basin has never had a book in which a printed day could be found by anybody who wanted to find it.** It is the first page in this basin that a stranger could use to find a day, and she entered that it happened and did not object.
- **The bench outside the door with the fanlight over it is not a room and is not entered**, and the district has no instrument for a person who is in a place and not in a book and did not make one. **A strip of paper in a coat is a document somebody owns. A page in a drawer is a page and not a copy. A copy a man made in fifty minutes is a copy. A word is not a document. None of the four is one of the three documents nobody owns.**
- **TWO System panels in ten chapters, at Chapters 207 and 210, and not three.** This line said three and named Chapter 205 as the third, and that was wrong: **205 is a stranger's printed nine-row schedule read out by a clerk, the same document shape as the lease in 203 and the list of five in 206, and on Volume 04's close test — a run of `> **` that is not introduced as a sheet somebody reads out — it is not a panel.** The block carries six such runs in four chapters and four of them are printed documents. 207 was on a lease and a holder and a bearer, and the man of about thirty-one disagreed with the whole of it; 210 was on a thing taken from a person who was not asked, and nobody disagreed with it. **No panel creates consent, makes a person a bearer, or makes a no hold.**

## The people, and what changed in the block

- **The man the figure of twenty-one years is against.** **The figure came off the ward market's own book on the thirty-first of March in the same line as the date and nothing was written in its place, and the district kept the label and the prose kept using it.** He is not named in the prose. **He was wrong twice in this block, both on the page.** He refused a bearer twice in a yard, on the fourth and the sixth, and it was never the thing the stranger came for. **On the eighth he asked the registrar for a name on a line for a trip with no term on it, and she refused in four seconds in eighty-two words, and he said afterwards that he knew the answer before he asked and asked in front of nineteen people anyway, and she said that is a thing clerks do and she did not enjoy it either.** **On the tenth he asked her for one thing and she granted it.** He has one departure left and has not spent it, the refusals to read stand at nine, the departure is unspent, and there is still no date in the ledger for the next reading.
- **The registrar of this district's records office, and the whole of her movement, is in the batch record at section 6.** She refuses in four seconds and gives the reason before she is asked, three times in this block. **She has refused in four seconds three times in eleven weeks, on the fifteenth of the second month, the second of April and the eighth of April.** What was said between the two of them on the seventh of April is in a minute in her own words if she chooses to write it and is not a document this district can produce if she does not.
- **The man of about thirty-four who digs loam.** **He says the plainest thing in the block and he refuses every join anybody offers him.** He refused the ninth place to the ninth holding, the fourth holding to the road, the eight days to the road, and the dray to the eight days. **He says in Chapter 209 that a run that goes one a night does not stop at eight because it is the eighth, and in Chapter 210 that he would like somebody to write down that he said he would not join two things and said it anyway, in his own words — and it was not written down, because the man it was asked of is a man of fifty-six and not a clerk.** He was asked nothing in any of the ten chapters and refused no ask, **because there is no ask to refuse.**
- **The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing.** **He asked the six things in nine of the ten chapters. On the eighth he said in this yard that he would ask the woman of fifty-eight himself in the morning and in his own words and not in the six things, and on the ninth he did not, and a clerk entered that he did not and that nobody asked him why.** On the ninth he carried two empty buckets up a hill for her and was not asked to, and counted from the bottom of the bank that she had been up it eleven times between about nine in the morning and about one in the afternoon, and was not asked to say he had counted. **He asked a man of about thirty at the counter for a word and got a word, and did not ask him for a man.**
- **The man of fifty-six.** Secretary of a cooperative of nine households, not an officer of anything, not a friend of anybody in that yard. He gives the figures out loud. **He is not a clerk and has said so about himself eleven times in four months, and at the end of this block a man asks him to write down the last true sentence anybody said about a dray going up a road and it cannot be written and nobody else writes it.** **The refusal of the two hundred yards and the two hundred yards of grade are his and are stated in the seventh and the eighth chapters and are a refusal and a grade and are not the refusal with no reason a clerk of a house has given.**
- **The man of about thirty-one who mends a dray at a forge end off Salt Row.** The fifth of the five posted securities and the fifth of the five things this district does not have. **On the third of April he came home with a strip of paper, about the length of his hand, with a line ruled across it and a word at the top and nothing under the line, and said in fifty-one words that the counter took his name off the sheet in about nine seconds, cost nothing, and asked nothing, and that he has not been released and has been found.** He had put a stub about the size of a coin in his coat on the first of April. **He said on the fifth, in forty-four words, that he does not know what a person at the back of a book is for, that he said on the thirtieth of the third month that it stops at him, and that he had four days to find out whether he meant it and did not.** He offered a dray on the seventh and said nobody has to pay him. **He took the yoke off the stones at about eleven on the ninth without asking, said so in fifty-four words, and asked for it in his own words. He put it on a tailboard in the open on the tenth because he had said he would not put it on a cart until she had seen it on a cart, and she did, and he said in fifty words that he has not got a way to pay her for the two days. He drove up the only road that climbs at about half past five on the eleventh.** **He is a man and not a bearer and is not in any of the three lists and was refused entry into all of them on the page.**
- **The woman of fifty-eight.** **Carried four yoke-loads a day up a hill for nine years. Her round of about ninety houses ended on the twenty-fourth of the third month because a water came down a channel, and this district arranged to take it off her and did not ask her and has nothing to give her in place of it.** On the first of April she said in sixty-four words that she cannot say what a day of it is worth and is not going to take a thing she cannot say the worth of. **A woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale offered her four houses on the thirty-first of the third month and has not repeated it and says four houses out of about ninety is not a round and is not going to become a round.** **On the ninth she said in sixty-five words that the yoke is hers and that she is not saying no to a man in a yard and is not saying yes and is telling the whole of what she knows and the whole of it is that.** On the tenth she stood at the bottom of a lane for about a minute and a quarter and said nothing, and on the tenth, put in the six things, she said no in four seconds in thirty-two words: *Then it is not mine to walk down. I have said for eleven days that I cannot say what a day of it is worth and this is a day of it.* **She has said the same shape of true sentence three times in eleven days to three different men. Nobody has priced a day. Her round is not coming back. The offer stands. The yoke is on a dray.**
- **The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale.** **She is the only person in this district who has ever been corrected by her own question in a yard, and she said so out loud in Chapter 205.** In Chapter 208 she said in fifty-four words that the yoke in this district is a carrying yoke with two hooks, that it has carried buckets for nine years, that it has never pulled anything in its life, and that a dray wants a draught yoke and this district has not got one. **She has weighed nine sacks a day for nine years and has never weighed a day, and that is the fifth of the five.**
- **The man of about thirty at the counter.** **Four sheets for a house thirty-four miles east, down the eleven mile road, not sent for, on the second, the fourth, the sixth and the ninth of April — four times in eight days, and this district has no schedule for him and never had one.** **He refused the ninth holding six times with no reason, the sixth of those on the fourth of April, and six are six. That six is not the pool: the pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given is seven, because five of his are inside the six Volume 04 reached on the twenty-fourth of the third month and his sixth is the seventh. The two figures are kept apart from this repair on.** He said on the sixth, in four seconds, that his house had never heard of a Registry. **On the ninth he gave the district the word locks in fifty-three words and did not say anything else and was not asked anything else.**
- **The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing.** **Owed eleven coppers a week, refused four times for the same reason, and the fourth refusal for the same reason is not a fourth refusal.** On the sixth of April he copied the schedule of nine rows in about fifty minutes onto the back of a sheet of his own buying, in his own hand, gave it to a clerk of nineteen years, and said twenty-one words: *I gave it up, nobody took it, put both of those down and put down which one of them is true.* **On the ninth of April that copy is the thing the eight days were read out of.** On the tenth he said he copied eight days and does not know what a lock is and would have copied it just the same and is not going to say that a fourth time. **He is owed eleven coppers a week and there is no money in this district that anybody can pay anybody with.**
- **The route keeper.** **Appears in person in Chapter 201, on his fourth walk in five weeks, nine miles, and says in forty-five words that somebody opened his road on the night of the twenty-second of the second month and that it was not him and that he has not seen who it was and is not going to say a name.** He is not in the yard again in this block. **Nobody sent for him about the eight days and the record says not asked.** He is not a bearer of a road and has never been asked to be one.
- **A man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households** — at the sluice end of the loams, not sent for, has been asked three times to be told what to do about three households and has not been told, and the yard decided on the nineteenth of the second month not to decide and has not gone back on it. **The six households stand at one signed, two refused, three not asked again, unchanged since the twenty-first of the third month.**
- **A woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat at the third house up the lane from the scale** — one of the two that refused, said in eighteen words on the third of April that she would rather be in the water with her name not written on anything than pay for it, and stood at her own door in the dark on the eleventh at half past five in the morning and watched the dray go, and was not asked anything and was not sent for. **She is in a pool of tolls with her name not written on anything and the not-asking is entered.**
- **A woman of about thirty-eight who keeps a building four miles down a lane** — refused in twenty-four words to tell a clerk of a house where her wall is, and the butt at the back of that building is at four days. Not asked anything in this block.
- **A man of about fifty-five who keeps that building** — **refused a fifth mend in the tank a third time, on the first of April, with the crack dry for the first time since the twenty-eighth of the second month, and gave no reason.** On the fifth of April he said in forty-six words that he has watched that tank for nine years and has never once been able to say where the water came from. **A tank with a mark on it is a tank he can see and a tank with a mend in it is a tank that comes out in three pieces.**
- **A man of about nineteen who counts** — the reason every figure in this block is checkable. **He is nobody's keeper and is not to be turned into one.**

## The counts, all of which held across the eleven days

**Things this district does not have five. Instruments built and not named six. Documents nobody owns three. Protected things five and no sixth. Conditions with no end on it four. Refusals to join two things thirteen and the registrar's own count twelve. Refusals to read nine, the departure unspent, and no date in the ledger for the next reading. **Refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given SEVEN and not six: the pool reached six in Volume 04, on the twenty-fourth of the third month in Chapter 198, and the seventh was given on the fourth of April in Chapter 204. This file said six until the review repair and was wrong, having taken the figure from Chapter 204. The refusals about the ninth holding by the man of about thirty are a separate figure and were five through the third of April and are six from the fourth; the two had been run together.** Boards five and lines full six, with the seventh line at the ninth place ruled and empty. Columns of not-askings four. A rate turning a year into coppers: none, and there never has been one.** **Nobody put a line on a board in any of the ten chapters. The eight days went into a book and not onto a board, and that is why the board figures did not move.** **The one thing that did move is not a count: a page in the registrar's office now holds eight printed days.**

## The counting motif, and the nineteen repairs

**This block carries forty-four word-count claims and all forty-four reproduce.** The method: a script matching *counted it and got N*, *counted them and got N*, *counted the reason and got N* and *counted the sentence and got N*, with the following printed bold sentence recounted by hand where the script could not resolve it, counting words separated by spaces with a hyphenated numeral as one word. **Three figures in the checkpointed Chapters 204, 205 and 206 were false and are repaired: Chapter 204's back-reference of forty-four words to the registrar's sentence, which is sixty-four and was said on the second of April; the three-way disagreement over Chapter 204's own refusal, which is sixty-seven words and was called fifty-two in Chapter 204's own restatement and in Chapter 206's quotation of it; and Chapter 205's twenty-six, where the sentence is twenty-five.** **Sixteen claims in the four new chapters were estimates and every one was low; all sixteen were corrected by recounting and not by shortening the prose.** **The lesson is on the record because it is the block's argument: a figure that is estimated instead of counted is not a figure.**

## Untouched in this block, to be left untouched

**The blank lot, Iven, the origin of the Common Measure, the First House, the removed signature, the three unexplained things of Volume 01, the office, the resolution of the romance, the name of the city on the agent's ledger, the name of the agent in any register in the west bank, the fourth line of the offer, the boy's name in a column in a building two days of road away, the two figures of five years and nine years, the iron, the echo of an origin toll, the Last Fare, the market stalls on the lower terrace, the first channel's failure and its sills, the four gaps, the two impressions, the two lines holding the office's name, the four lines of the broken circle, the signal's memory pattern, the pan that cannot report itself, the place that cannot report itself, and the mark on the iron. No place is named on any document in this district and none is named in narration. The Crownless Uplands, the record caves, the Lock Council, the Upland assemblies, the Guilds, Selik Marne and the winter market are not in this block.**

# Volume 05 continuity — Batch 0002, Chapters 211 to 220

**The eighteenth year after the Long Fracture, the twelfth to the twenty-first of the fourth month. One chapter to a day, ten days, ten chapters. The month turned once in Volume 05, in Chapter 201, and did not turn again here. This section is additive; nothing in it corrects a Volume 04 record except where a figure is named, and where a figure is named the chapter is the authority.**

## The world in this block

- **The dray went up the only road in this district that climbs at about half past five on the eleventh of April and came down it at about half past three in the afternoon on the fourteenth with ONE man on the box.** The man with the portfolio was not on it and the case was not on it, and a clerk of nineteen years entered that a man who goes up a road and does not come down it in a cart has not refused to come down and cannot be counted either way, and that the record says not asked. **The yoke was on the tailboard and it came back down with the dray.**
- **The standing orders of the nine locks.** A single printed sheet, nine rows, **a number in a column on the left and a night in a column on the right and a column headed KEEPER between them.** Nine nights: **the first, second, third, fourth, sixth, eighth, tenth, eleventh and twentieth of April.** **The column headed KEEPER carries a house on every one of the nine rows and a person on none of them.** It came down on the dray on the fourteenth, folded, under the yoke, and it was not on the cart when the cart went up. **The man of about thirty-one was handed it on the road by a person who did not give a name and did not read it and has not been asked about it since.**
- **The stranger's schedule** is unchanged and is the first eight nights of the standing orders in the same order, with **IT IS NOT A ROAD** where the standing orders have the twentieth. **Its ninth row carries a word and not a day and its sheet has no column for a keeper at all.** The block's finding: **two lists of nine that agree on eight are not one list, and the one they disagree about is the ninth.**
- **The road house's book.** At a road house at the second place, about the length of a forearm, **written in more than one hand**, carrying the eight nights the man who keeps that road has walked the road in this run: **the first, second, third, fifth, seventh, ninth, twelfth and thirteenth of April.** He refused the ninth in forty-two words with a reason — *that one is a night another man wrote and not a night I walked, and I keep a road and I am not a keeper of a book, and I am not taking a man's page round a yard like this one* — **so it is not one of the refusals with no reason.** He says the road house's book is a record of when a man walked and a column headed OPEN is a record of something this district cannot see, and that **two records that are not about the same thing cannot agree and cannot disagree.**
- **The two-column sheet, read out in Chapter 214 and the checkable arithmetic of the block.** Three in both. Five in the road house book and not on the printed sheet: the fifth, seventh, ninth, twelfth, thirteenth. Six on the printed sheet and not in the book: the fourth, sixth, eighth, tenth, eleventh, twentieth. **Of the five, three are the three days of eleven a stranger's column headed OPEN leaves empty, and two are after the last day any printed column in this district carries.**
- **The first letter this district has had from a council.** One sheet with a seal, sent to the people of the place this district is. It **apologises for the first of these coming four months late**, says the council is a body and not a place and does not say where it sits or when it sits again, says nine things hold back a water above four districts and no person holds any of them, **asks whether this district will send a man to a council, and asks for an answer by the last day of this month.** It sat unopened in the district for two days and was read out in a yard on the sixteenth of April by the man the figure of twenty-one years is against, and **it was read and not discussed.**
- **The finding about keepers, and it is the block's answer to the question the phase prompt left open.** **No keeper is named on a page, and no keeper has ever been on a page of anybody's, and the reason is not that pages forget people: it is that the page has a column and the column carries a house.** Three bodies keep a page in this basin and not one of them has ever written a keeper on any of them: the keepers, for four generations; the Registry; and this district's own records office. **Those three are the same sentence told three times and are one finding and not three.**
- **The tank four miles down a lane.** The butt at the back of the brick building was at four days on the first of April and is at nine days on the twentieth of April, **which is nineteen days**, and the tank within a hand's breadth of its lip is the second time since the first of the second month, and the crack is running. **A man of about fifty-five who keeps that building refused a fifth mend in about two seconds and gave a reason, and a clerk of nineteen years entered that she is not going to count how many times he has said no to a fifth mend.**
- **The fourth holding on the delivery sheet at the second reservoir took a drop on the night of the nineteenth into the twentieth of April, for the first time since the first of the second month, and the channel at the head of the top field has about a foot of water in it.** The holding is the top field of a farm four miles out on the only road that climbs. **A man of about thirty-four who digs loam opened a sluice for it on the night of the thirteenth of last month, thirty-six days before the eighteenth, and nobody has asked him to open it again and he says he has not.** **He called it the third time this month a wet thing had been put next to a night nine miles off, said that a third time is a habit, and said he would not let a pail of water be the witness. It is not joined to the road a route keeper says somebody opened on the night of the twenty-second of the second month, and it is not joined to the ninth of the nine nights.**

## The instruments, and the one this block found out about the yard

- **A yard is a door, and this block proved the other half of Batch 0001's finding on the page.** A man of fifty-six said in Chapter 216 that this yard took a job off a woman in the third month without asking her and spent the twenty-four days afterwards calling the not-asking a column, **and that a yard that about nineteen people can stand in and decide something about a woman who is not in it is a door, and that the door which got used last month was not the one anybody was proud of, and that this morning the same yard is deciding something about the same woman in the same room and the whole of the difference is that she is standing in it.** A man of about thirty-four who digs loam said he wanted the asking written down as the whole of the reason and not the eleven.
- **The registrar's office is a room and is the only instrument in this district that holds a record.** On the twenty-first she said out loud, unasked, in fifty-one words, **that her book now holds eight printed days off a stranger's sheet and nine printed nights off a body's own paper, and that a person may look at both of them in that office and may not carry either of them out of it.**
- **Two System panels in ten chapters, at Chapters 216 and 218, and not three.** Four further runs of `> **` in the block are printed documents and are not panels: a road house's book in Chapter 212, the standing orders in Chapter 213, a clerk's two-column sheet in Chapter 214, and the council's letter in Chapter 215. **The reliable test in this volume: a document is introduced as a thing somebody reads out and is read back because it is the rule of the counter; a panel arrives unasked and is then argued with or not argued with.** Chapter 216's panel was disagreed with out loud by the woman it is about; Chapter 218's was disagreed with by nobody. **No panel creates consent, makes a person a bearer, or makes a no hold.**

## The people, and what changed in the block

- **The man the figure of twenty-one years is against.** **Refused in seventy-eight words, in the six things, in front of about nineteen people, to have this district's answer to the council decided by a yard** — *I am not going to say no in a yard about a thing nobody has asked me properly, and a body that wants a man out of this district has to come to this district and put the question to me in a room with a clerk in it, and I am not going to be a man who is sent to a council because about nineteen people stood in a yard and somebody read out a letter.* **A refusal with a reason, and it did not touch the count of eight.** He read the council's letter out in a yard. He gave her a different page in Chapter 217. **He has not been in a room with the registrar since the fourth of April.**
- **The registrar of this district's records office.** **One movement in this block and it is not a declaration: in a lane behind the forge end off Salt Row, with no clerk within nine feet, she asked him for the page of the ward market's own book where the figure of twenty-one years came off on the thirty-first of last month, in his own hand, to put in her own book, and he gave her the other side of the same leaf, which carries the date and his own hand and nothing written under it, and said that it is a page and it is not the one she asked for.** Nobody counted either sentence. **She refused nothing and granted nothing in these ten chapters. She has refused in four seconds three times in eight weeks and four days — the fifteenth of the second month, the second of April and the eighth — and there was no fourth.** She was in the yard on six days running in Chapter 211 and was not asked a first thing on any of them. **The romance is unresolved.**
- **The man of about thirty-four who digs loam.** Said the sum out loud on the twelfth and said a run that goes one a night does not stop at eight because it is the eighth. **Refused to put the road house's book next to the stranger's sheet and gave the reason.** Put two documents next to each other in a yard in Chapter 213 and said so. **Announced his own resemblance about the tank twice before anybody could find it.** Refused the fourth-holding join a third time in twelve days. **Said on the nineteenth that he has disagreed with one panel in fourteen days and has not disagreed with one, and that the one he has not disagreed with is the one about a person, and that he was not going to say any more about that.** **Asked three times in ten days for a sentence of his own to be written down and was not written down three times.**
- **The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing.** **Kept the promise Batch 0001 left open: on the sixteenth of April he walked up the bank at about half past eight with nothing in his hands and asked the woman of fifty-eight, in his own words and not in the six things, how many of the ninety houses she can still name, and she said eleven.** On the eighth of this month he named a day out loud in a yard and the day arrived. **Said in ninety-six words that no keeper has ever been on a page and it is not because pages forget people.** Said in fifty-four words that a body that writes to a village is a body that knows the village has something. **He did not carry the buckets up the bank on the nineteenth and a man of fifty-six entered that a man who has stopped doing a thing he was never asked to do has not refused anything and the record says not asked.**
- **The man of fifty-six.** Gives the figures out loud, reads the board out and gets it right, and is not a clerk and has said so about himself eleven times in four months. **Named the yard as a door and said the door which got used last month was not the one anybody was proud of.** Said that a letter is a document this district has got and did not write.
- **The man of about thirty-one who mends a dray at a forge end off Salt Row.** **Came down the road on the fourteenth with the yoke and a printed sheet under it and the strip of paper back on a trestle table, unasked, with nobody asking him about it.** Refused in seventy words to say who gave him the sheet, refused in fifty-four words to say whether the house in the keeper's column is the same house nine times or nine houses, and refused in seventy words to say where the road goes — **all three with a reason, so none of them is in the count of eight.** **Went up the road again at about half past five on the twentieth with a yoke left in the forge and was not back on the twenty-first.**
- **The woman of fifty-eight.** **Eleven of about ninety still have a door on them, and she said so on the sixteenth and refused in seventy-three words to have the eleven names said in a yard.** Took eleven houses of the round on the seventeenth in four seconds in sixty words without pricing them. **Disagreed with a panel out loud in fifty-one words — the third person in the volume to disagree with one.** Split eleven into six above the channel and five below it. Said on the nineteenth that she will do eleven, one at a time, eleven times, and on the twenty-first that she started at seven in the morning and that this is the first morning anybody has left it alone. **Nobody has priced a day. The round of about seventy-nine is not coming back and about ninety is not a figure so that is not a subtraction.**
- **The man with the portfolio.** **Not back.** The fourteenth would have been his thirteenth day and a clerk entered that a day a man is not here is not a day he has been here. He is off the page from Chapter 214 onward.
- **The man of about thirty at the counter.** **The fifteenth time down the eleven mile road, on the fifteenth of April, a hundred and three days after the second of January, which is fourteen weeks and five days. The whole run is named and dated on the page: eleventh on the second, twelfth on the fourth, thirteenth on the sixth, fourteenth on the ninth, fifteenth today.** He refused the ninth holding for the seventh time with no reason. He said in thirty-eight words that a house is what keeps a lock when a man cannot and that nobody would tell him a name. He did not come on the twenty-first and the record says not asked.
- **The route keeper.** **Walked nine miles on the thirteenth, gave eight nights, refused the ninth with a reason in forty-two words, gave the district two more words in fifty, said in forty-six words that he walks the road when there is water in a channel and that that is not a keeper's reason, and said in thirty-six words that he was nine miles off on the eleventh and that there was no wheel track on the road.** He is not a bearer of a road and has never been asked to be one.
- **The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing.** **Copied the standing orders in about forty minutes on the twenty-first, gave the copy to a clerk, and said twelve words: *Nobody took it. I gave it away. Put both of those down.*** Said on the twelfth that he does not know what a lock is and has copied the word nine times. **Owed eleven coppers a week, refused four times for the same reason, and the fourth refusal for the same reason is not a fourth refusal.**
- **The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale** — asked what four houses of a round is worth a day for the third time in thirteen days and gave the same answer for the third time. **The four houses still stand and have not been taken up and four and eleven were refused entry as an addition on the page.**
- **The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households** — said in fifty-two words that the house has to ask him, that he has been asked four times and has not been told, and that a fourth time is not a fifth. **The six stand at one signed, two refused, three not asked again, unchanged since the twenty-first of last month.**
- **The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat** — was in the yard on the eighteenth, was not asked, and said in twenty-one words that she stood in the cold because she was awake and that the clerk is not to make it into anything. **The woman of about thirty-eight who keeps a building four miles down a lane** — said in thirty-nine words that the butt is for the water off the roof, is four days, was nine, and that she has not asked anybody for a fifth day and is not going to. **The man of about fifty-five who keeps that building** — refused a fifth mend in about two seconds with a reason on the twentieth. **A man of about nineteen who counts** — the reason every figure in this block is checkable, and he counted forty-five sentences in these ten chapters and all forty-five reproduce. He is nobody's keeper and is not to be turned into one.

## The counts, and the one that moved

**Things this district does not have five. Instruments built and not named six. Documents nobody owns three, and both new documents were refused entry with a stated reason. Protected things five and no sixth. Conditions with no end on it four. Refusals to join two things thirteen and the registrar's own count twelve, and neither moved, and a second refusal of the same join is the same refusal and the block says so four times on the page. Refusals to read nine, the departure unspent, and no date in the ledger for the next reading. Boards five and lines full six, with the seventh line at the ninth place ruled and empty, and nobody put a line on a board in any of the ten chapters — the ninth of April's printed date was offered to the board and refused on the ground that a date is not a finding. Columns of not-askings four, and no fifth was ruled. A rate turning a year into coppers: none, and there never has been one.**

**REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN ARE EIGHT AND NOT SEVEN, AND THEY MOVED ON THE FIFTEENTH OF APRIL ON THE PAGE.** The pool reached six in Volume 04 on the twenty-fourth of last month, the seventh was given on the fourth of April in Chapter 204, and **the eighth was given on the fifteenth of April in Chapter 214 by the man of about thirty at the counter refusing the ninth holding for the seventh time with no reason. That is the first movement since the twenty-fourth of last month, which is twenty-two days.** **The refusals about the ninth holding are a separate figure: six through the fourteenth of April and seven from the fifteenth.** The two are not added together and must not be. **A ninth must not be entered without a reason on the page.**

## The counting motif, forty-five claims and the forty-four repairs

**This block carries forty-five word-count claims and all forty-five reproduce.** The method: a script matching *counted it and got N*, *counted them and got N*, *counted the reason and got N* and *counted the sentence and got N*, resolving the following printed bold sentence and counting words separated by spaces with a hyphenated numeral as one word. **Forty-four of the forty-five were drafted as estimates, forty-three of the forty-four were low and one was high**, the same result Batch 0001 got on sixteen of its own four new chapters; all forty-four were corrected by recounting and not by shortening the prose. **Seven intervals that were remembered spans rather than counted ones were also corrected, and two all-caps restatements that quoted a figure the chapter no longer held were corrected with them.** A first pass of the script returned six false mismatches because *six* matched the first three letters of *sixty*; the detector was repaired and the prose was not touched. **The lesson is on the record a second time because it is the block's argument: a figure that is estimated instead of counted is not a figure, and the estimation was low every single time, which is what estimation is.**

## Untouched in this block, to be left untouched

**The blank lot, Iven, the origin of the Common Measure, the First House, the removed signature, the three unexplained things of Volume 01, the office, the resolution of the romance, the name of the city on the agent's ledger, the name of the agent in any register in the west bank, the fourth line of the offer, the boy's name in a column in a building two days of road away, the two figures of five years and nine years, the iron, the echo of an origin toll, the Last Fare, the market stalls on the lower terrace, the first channel's failure and its sills, the four gaps, the two impressions, the two lines holding the office's name, the four lines of the broken circle, the signal's memory pattern, the pan that cannot report itself, the place that cannot report itself, and the mark on the iron. No place is named on any document in this district and none is named in narration. Cael Orin was not said a second time. The record caves, the founder's chair, the nine design signatures, the tolls arriving in villages that did not sign, the nine local charters, the regional covenant and the ninth signature are not in this block. The Crownless Uplands, the Guilds, Selik Marne and the winter market are not in this block and none is named. The volume's midpoint reversal is not spent here and nothing in these ten chapters foreshadows it.**

---

# Volume 05 continuity — Batch 0003, Chapters 221 to 230

**The eighteenth year after the Long Fracture, the twenty-second of April to the second of the fifth month. Ten chapters on eleven days, with Chapter 229 carrying the thirtieth of April and the first of the fifth month together. The month turned in the night of the thirtieth and was said out loud once, on the second. This section is additive; nothing in it corrects a Volume 04 or Volume 05 Batch 0002 record except where a figure is named, and where a figure is named the chapter is the authority.**

## The world in this block

- **The Council's offer.** A second sheet, dated the eighteenth of April, lying on the trestle table in the yard of Lot Seventeen on the morning of the twenty-second with nobody in the yard having put it there. It is four lines and it offers a village **one sheet, one holder, one term and one name**; the holder is to sit in a chair, the chair is to be called a founder's chair, and the term is a term with no end on it; the nine things that hold back the water will be held by the one holder for the four districts under them and the offer will work from tomorrow; an answer is asked for by the last day of that month. **It has a place ruled for a name with nothing in it.** It was read out in that yard on the twenty-second and read back afterwards and is a document this district has and did not write and cannot produce, and is not a fourth of the three documents nobody owns.
- **Two refusals of the chair, and they are not the same refusal.** **In a room with a shut door in it on the twenty-fourth, in a hundred and seventy words:** a single holder cannot be asked a question by anybody, a charter with no end on it that begins tomorrow decides what is sown in the season after this one, the four districts under that water would not be asked about either, there is nobody in that room to ask, and a name at the back of a book is a person a toll can be fastened to. **In a yard on the twenty-eighth, in the six things, in front of about nineteen people, in a hundred and sixty-two words:** a yard is not a body and nineteen people standing in the cold are not a council, and if he took the chair off the table in front of them it would be because they were there and not because a man said no to a piece of paper, and he will not be a founder because a yard is full. **He said the whole of the room reason again in the yard on the twenty-fourth afternoon, on purpose, so that one of the two repetitions would be in a book.**
- **A chair stood in that yard from the twenty-third to the twenty-fourth.** It was carried in on its back by a man of about twenty-three who does not keep anything and was not sent for, nobody sat in it, it was moved four feet once, and a clerk of nineteen years entered that a piece of furniture in a yard is a piece of furniture and is not one of the six instruments and did not become a seventh, and that a chair nobody has sat in is not a founder's chair.
- **THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL, spent in Chapters 224 to 227 and nowhere else.** **A page in a Registry's hand carries the name of the man of about thirty-one who mends a dray against this district's records office and says he serves it. The name was entered on the second of April, the day he gave a name at a counter that takes names off sheets. He read the page on the twenty-second of April, in the room where the books are, with the registrar in the room, while carrying a ledger to a counter because the man who keeps that counter was not well. He has carried things for that office nine or ten times and has never been given a job for it and has never been paid a penny of it. He said nothing for three days and then disclosed it himself: in a yard on the twenty-sixth and in a room with a shut door in it on the night of the twenty-fifth.** He loses nothing that was his. He loses the right to be a stranger four hundred yards from his own forge, the ledger work, a day of his labour, and the ability to have the name taken off: **a name put on a figure cannot be taken off it by the person it is put on, and saying it out loud made the name harder to get rid of, and he said in twenty-five words that he thought somebody would take it off, that he was wrong, that he knew it was wrong, and that he said it anyway.**
- **The registrar may not carry that page out of her own office** and said so in eighty words, on the ground that the rule is hers, that she wrote it on the twenty-first, and that she wrote it before she knew there was anything on it. She then entered four lines in her own book that do not carry the name.
- **A third column headed with a word and not a name, and it carries a man once.** The first carries a house nine times and a person no times; this one carries the man of about thirty-one.
- **The work did not stop. It stopped for one man and went somewhere else.** A ledger of nineteen rows and four columns, about as much as a full bucket, on a table four feet high under a hatch in a counter room nine feet by eleven, two hundred yards each way, on the days a man of about nineteen is not well four days a month. It has not gone since the twenty-second. **Nobody has been asked to carry it, and the record says not asked.** A woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat carried one on the twenty-second because it was in her arms, and said she is not going to say yes and is not going to say no, and asked for the not-asking to be written down, and it was written down, and a clerk entered that a request for a thing to be written down is not a column.
- **Four men went up the only road in this district that climbs on the twenty-ninth** — the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing, the man of about thirty-four who digs loam, the man of about twenty-three who copies for nothing and the man of about thirty-one who mends a dray, who was asked and said yes in about four seconds because he is the only one of the four who can find the turn. They took a lamp, a slate, a pencil and about nine feet of line. **The climb is about four miles and the last half mile of it is not a road.**
- **A house at a road nine miles up has kept a thing in the water for four generations and cannot be asked who kept it.** It will let four men in and hold a slate and will not let a page out. **A page is copied and not carried, and the caves have never been emptied.** The first answer to the question who kept them came in eleven words and is printed on the page, and it went into no minute and no book and was not counted because the man of about nineteen was not there. The second answer, asked in the man's own words, was fifty-six words: **we have not had a name for him in four generations and it is not that we have lost it, it is that we have never had it, and there was never a page with his name on it, and there is a page with our house on it.**
- **THE PAGE IN THE CAVE.** A hand copy, made in about fifty minutes in a doorway with a lamp on the step, in a man of about twenty-three's own hand, with his left hand on the slate and his right on the pencil. **Nine places, eight names, one rule: NO PERSON SHALL HOLD THIS ALONE. And under the rule, in a second hand, one sentence underlined twice: A MAN WHO IS THANKED FOR THIS HAS BEEN MADE SOMETHING ELSE, AND THE SOMETHING IS NOT A CHAIR.** The ninth place is ruled and empty. **The eight names were not read out in the doorway and were not read out in a yard, and the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said the ruled ninth place is the same shape of nothing as the seventh line at the ninth place on the board outside the room, and said so before anybody else did.**
- **The tank four miles down a lane is over its lip** and there are two chalk lines on the inside of the brick, the second above the one drawn on the seventeenth. A man of about fifty-five who keeps that building put nine years of beds in a second room at about two in the morning on the twenty-fifth and told nobody until the morning, which is what he said on the twentieth he would do. He has still not asked for a fifth mend and the crack is running. A woman of about thirty-eight who keeps that building sent word by a boy of about nine that the beds were in the second room and the water had not come over the step.
- **The lease was not answered on the last day of the fourth month** and a clerk of nineteen years entered that a lease that has not been answered on the day it falls due has not been refused and has not been taken up and has not been given up, and that she is not going to enter a refusal on a lease nobody in this district was ever asked about. **The answer to the Council went up the only road in this district that climbs at about half past five on the first of the fifth month, with the man of about thirty-one on the box, who was going up anyway, and who signed nothing: the man the figure of twenty-one years is against wrote the four sentences himself and put his own name at the bottom of them and nobody asked him to.**

## The instruments, and the two this block found out about the door and the page

- **A shut door is the only place this district has found where a person can be told no to a thing and have the no be true, and a yard is the only place it has found where nineteen people can hear him say it, and the man has now been in both.** Said in sixty-eight words in Chapter 223 and entered as two instruments in a week and neither of them with a name.
- **A room with a shut door in it is a place where a sentence goes to die and not a book.** A clerk cannot enter what was said in a room she was not standing in, and the registrar's rule of the twenty-first — that a person may look at a document in that office and may not carry it out — is the reason the Registry's page cannot be produced and is the reason the most honest disclosure this district has had is not on a page.
- **Two System panels in ten chapters, at Chapters 222 and 226, and not three.** Three further runs of `> **` in the block are printed documents and are not panels: the Council's offer in Chapter 221, the answer in Chapter 229 and the copy of the cave page in Chapter 230. Chapter 222's panel was disagreed with by nobody; Chapter 226's was disagreed with out loud by the man of about thirty-four who digs loam, **and that panel's third line is the volume's own reversal, that a disclosure is an instrument and prevents a thing being done without a person knowing.** No panel creates consent, makes a person a bearer or makes a no hold.

## The people, and what changed in the block

- **The man the figure of twenty-one years is against.** **Refused the chair twice, in a room and in a yard, in different words, on different grounds, and the second refusal exists because of the first.** He asked for a clerk in the room on the twenty-fourth because he had asked for one in a yard on the nineteenth. He said the room reason again in the yard on purpose so that one of the two would be in a book. He wrote the answer to the Council himself and signed it. **The romance did not move in this block and that is deliberate.**
- **The registrar of this district's records office.** **No movement in the romance.** She put the offer to him herself in her own office and did not read it out of the sheet. She said the clerk is nineteen and is at the ward market and that a person in a room is not a clerk and cannot enter anything. She refused to carry the Registry's page out of her own office in eighty words. She entered four lines that do not carry the name and said that is the whole of what that room can do and it is not nothing. **She refused nothing and granted nothing in these ten chapters.**
- **The man of about thirty-four who digs loam.** **His body cost him something.** He put a spade into a wet bank on the twenty-second and could not lift a spade above his head for the rest of the block, and announced it before anybody could ask him. He carried a lamp up a four-mile climb in his right hand on the twenty-ninth and could not get his fingers to close on it for about a minute at the bottom. He asked a fourth time in eleven days for a sentence of his own to be written down, said out loud that it was not going to happen so that the not-happening would be a thing the yard did, and was not written down. **On the thirtieth, without asking, a clerk of nineteen years wrote down what he said in his own words, and he said it is not the same thing and it is the best that has happened to me in a month and he is not going to say thank you in front of nine people.** He said the third-column finding in a hundred and thirty-eight words, the work-did-not-stop finding in eighty-five words, refused a fifth join, and **disagreed with a panel out loud for the second time in the volume.** He got the spade above his head in two goes on the second of the fifth month and nobody counted that either.
- **The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing.** Kept the two things he said he would do. Put the chair question himself in the six things. Read the second sheet out three times in three days and the answer out before it went and the board out on the thirtieth without being asked. Sat on a step in the cold from half past five until nine on the twenty-fifth with nothing in his hands. **Said the block's summary in sixty-eight words.**
- **The man of fifty-six.** Gives the figures out loud, reads the board and is not a clerk. **Said that a yard which never sends anybody to a room only ever gets the answers it deserves and that a yard that does send somebody to a room gets a different man back in the afternoon.** That a rule a person makes for herself and then uses against somebody else's harm is the only instrument this district has built and not named in four months. That a man counting his own unpaid labour is keeping a tally and not a clerk. In nine of the ten chapters.
- **The man of about thirty-one who mends a dray.** **This is the block.** Came down the road on the twenty-fifth after five days with a yoke still in a forge. Asked for a room and not a yard. Disclosed the Registry's page himself, in a yard and then in a room. Said in seventy-nine words that he did the work and was never given the job and never paid a penny of it, and in twenty-five words that he thought somebody would take his name off it. **Lost nothing that was his and paid for saying it. Is a man and not a bearer, is not a clerk of a house, is not one of the five things this district does not have, and is not on the standing orders of the nine.**
- **The woman of fifty-eight.** Three reactions and one refusal. In seventy-five words on the twenty-second she said nobody has ever asked her once whether she would rather somebody else carried it, and that she knows which of two things she has been for nine years, and would not say it in front of nine people — and then went out of the yard and up the bank with two buckets. In a hundred and six words on the twenty-seventh she used the yard's own rule about a name said twice to protect a man who is not hers. In thirty-nine words on the twenty-eighth she refused to hear a letter about a chair read out. **On the second of the fifth month, uncounted, she said she has not been thanked once in ten days and did not want to be, and that it is the first time in nine years a thing she has done has gone unwritten and she finds she does not mind.** The eleventh bucket went up the bank at about ten past twelve and nobody counted the buckets. **Nobody has priced a day. The round of about seventy-nine is not coming back. The offer of four houses still stands.**
- **The man with the portfolio.** **Still not in this district, off the page, and nothing in this block says where he is and nothing cheapens his return.** The lease was not answered on the day it fell due.
- **The man of about thirty at the counter.** **The sixteenth time down the eleven mile road, on the twenty-third of April, a hundred and eleven days after the second of January, which is fifteen weeks and six days. The whole run is named and dated on the page: eleventh on the second, twelfth on the fourth, thirteenth on the sixth, fourteenth on the ninth, fifteenth on the fifteenth, sixteenth today.** He was **not** asked about the ninth holding, and a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said out loud in ninety-five words that he was not going to. **The pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given stands at EIGHT and did not move, and the refusals about the ninth holding stand at SEVEN and did not move, and the two are not the same figure.** He gave four words at a gate as he was leaving on the twenty-third and nobody heard them and a man who mends fencing entered them the next morning: **a house at a road nine miles up will let a page be copied and will not let a page be carried.** He had asked twice in thirty-four miles and had never been given an answer and had stopped asking, and he gave a word and not a name and had never been given a name either. The next is the **seventeenth**.
- **The route keeper.** Walked through that yard at about one in the afternoon on the thirtieth without stopping, not sent for, not asked a first thing. He has been asked one question in this district since the first of this month and has given one answer.
- **The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing.** **Copied the page from the cave in about fifty minutes in a doorway with a lamp on the step and went home with his hand open.** **Refused to read the eight names out in the doorway and in a yard, in his own words, on this district's own rule about a name said twice, which he learned off a woman of fifty-eight in the last fortnight.** Third copy in a month. Owed eleven coppers a week, refused four times for the same reason, and the district is not going to pay him and has now said so four times. He has said he will not say it a fifth and did not.
- **The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale** — asked what a chair is worth for the **fourth time in eighteen days**, gave the same answer for the fourth time in sixty-three words, and said she was going to stop being asked and start being told. **Her four houses still stand and were not added to eleven.**
- **The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households** — said in seventy words that the house has to ask him, that he has been asked five times and has not been told, that the six have not moved since the twenty-first of last month which is forty days, and that five is not a sixth and a fourth time was not a fifth either. **The six stand at one signed, two refused, three not asked again.**
- **The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat** — asked whether she would carry a ledger two hundred yards each way and said in eighty-three words that she carried one on the twenty-second without knowing it was a job, that it was a Friday and she did not know that either, that she is not going to say yes or no, and that she wants it written down that nobody asked her. **The first time in four months a person in that yard has asked for the not-asking to be written down.**
- **The man of about fifty-five who keeps a building four miles down a lane** — the tank is over its lip, there are two chalk lines on the inside of the brick, nine years of beds are in a second room, the crack is running, and he has still not asked for a fifth mend. Said in sixty-six words on the twenty-fifth that he did what he said he would do at about two in the morning and told nobody until the morning, and that he has not lost one of nine years of beds, and that the water is where the water comes from and he is fifty-five and has stopped expecting to be told.
- **The woman of about thirty-eight who keeps that building** — at the end of that lane with a pail, not asked a first thing, and her word carried by a boy of about nine who is not a bearer and is not a clerk and is not anybody's instrument.
- **A man of about nineteen who counts** — counted forty-five sentences in these ten chapters resolved from a claim phrase to the sentence printed under it, and nine more counted spans in the form *in N words*, and all fifty-four reproduce. He has been counting since the second of April, which is twenty-one days, and was asked what he counts and why and said he does not know why the number mattered. He is nobody's keeper and is not to be turned into one.

## The counts, and the one that did not move

**Things this district does not have five. Instruments built and not named six, and eight candidates were refused a seventh on the page. Documents nobody owns three, and five documents arrived or were made in this block and none became a fourth. Protected things five and no sixth. Conditions with no end on it four, and a term with no end on it printed on a sheet was refused a fifth in eighty-three words. Refusals to join two things thirteen and the registrar's own count twelve, and neither moved, and a fifth refusal of the same join was refused a sixth entry on the page. Refusals to read nine, the departure unspent, and no date in the ledger for the next reading. Boards five and lines full six, with the seventh line at the ninth place ruled and empty, and nobody put a line on a board on any of the ten days. Columns of not-askings four, and no fifth was ruled and a woman asked for the not-asking to be written down and it was not a column. A rate turning a year into coppers: none, and there never has been one.**

**THE POOL OF REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN IS EIGHT AND THE REFUSALS ABOUT THE NEINTH HOLDING ARE SEVEN, AND BOTH STOOD WHERE THEY WERE ON EVERY ONE OF THE TEN DAYS.** The man of about thirty at the counter came for the sixteenth time on the twenty-third and **was not asked about the ninth holding, and the yard said out loud in ninety-five words that it was not going to ask him, because an eighth asking in a yard turns the eight into the ninth for asking and not for refusing.** The pool last moved on the twenty-fourth of last month, which is the twenty-fourth of the third month, which is thirty-seven days before the thirtieth of April. **A ninth must not be entered without a reason on the page.**

## The counting motif, fifty-four claims, twenty-seven of them estimates in the first draft

**This block carries fifty-four word-count claims and all fifty-four reproduce, in two classes. CLASS ONE, forty-five: the claim is the number word captured by a claim phrase — *counted it and got*, *was counted and got*, *it came to* — in the narration immediately before a printed sentence standing in a quoted paragraph, and it is resolved forward to that paragraph. Per chapter, four in 221, four in 222, two in 223, three in 224, seven in 225, six in 226, four in 227, four in 228, four in 229, seven in 230. CLASS TWO, nine counted spans in the form *in N words*, also resolved forward to a printed paragraph: 223 a hundred and seventy, 225 twenty-five, 228 four words at a gate, 228 eleven words out of a doorway, 228 seven words about a page, 229 four words carried by a boy of about nine. In both classes the sentence is counted with words separated by spaces, a hyphenated numeral taken as one word, and the trailing full stop not counted. A claim with no printed paragraph under it is not a figure: six such spans were found by the review of this block and every one of them has been printed — the four words at a gate, the seven words about a page, the eleven words out of a doorway, the four words a boy carried, the twenty-eight words about a ninth place, and the hundred and seventy words in a shut room — and two figures were set to what the prose says, the twenty-five words in Chapter 225 and the hundred and seventy in Chapter 223. **Thirty-three printed quoted paragraphs in the block carry no count on the page and that is entered rather than passed.** **Twenty-seven of the claims were drafted as estimates in the first draft and all twenty-seven were wrong, twenty-two low and five high; all twenty-seven were corrected by recounting the printed sentence and not by shortening the prose, and the claims added afterwards were written from the measurement and were right on entry.** Three detector faults were found while verifying and none of them was a fault in the prose: a number-word alternation with no tens in it, so that *seventy-five* matched as *seven*; a loose match on the first *got* in a line rather than the *got* inside a claim phrase; and a hyphenated numeral counted as two words in a first pass. **The lesson is on the record a third time because it is the block's argument: a figure that is estimated instead of counted is not a figure.**

## Untouched in this block, to be left untouched

**The blank lot, Iven, the origin of the Common Measure, the First House, the removed signature, the three unexplained things of Volume 01, the office, the resolution of the romance, the name of the city on the agent's ledger, the name of the agent in any register in the west bank, **the fourth line of the offer**, the boy's name in a column in a building two days of road away, the two figures of five years and nine years, the iron, the echo of an origin toll, the Last Fare, the market stalls on the lower terrace, the first channel's failure and its sills, the four gaps, the two impressions, the two lines holding the office's name, the four lines of the broken circle, the signal's memory pattern, the pan that cannot report itself, the place that cannot report itself, and the mark on the iron. No place is named on any document in this district and none is named in narration. Cael Orin was not said a third time. The ninth design signature belongs to Iven Tallow and he is not in the volume, does not appear, and is not named or foreshadowed. The tolls arriving in villages that did not sign, the nine local charters, the regional covenant, the Council's letter calling this district's man a founder on nine walls and the Convener promotion are not in this block and are Chapters 231 onward. Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block.**
