# Volume 05 continuity — Batch 0004, Chapters 231 to 240

**The eighteenth year after the Long Fracture, the third to the twelfth of the fifth month. Ten chapters on ten days, one chapter to a day, and no chapter carries two dates, which is the first block in this volume where that is true. The month turned in the night of the thirtieth of April and was said out loud once, on the second, in Chapter 230, and it was not said again. This section is additive; nothing in it corrects a Volume 04, Volume 05 Batch 0002 or Volume 05 Batch 0003 record, and where a figure is named the chapter is the authority.**

## The world in this block

- **A TOLL SHEET, on the trestle table on the morning of the third of the fifth month, folded once, put there by nobody in that yard.** A body that keeps records of holdings sends a toll on a gate to each of four places by the roads below the water; the toll on the gate is one toll paid by the party of each of the four and by nobody else; a gate holds a road shut and a road below a gate is a road that is shut; the four are numbered and not named; **a place is the fourth of the four by itself until somebody says it is not**; the toll is paid once and is not paid twice, and the body is not asked whether a gate has been paid. **It names four parties and not one person and it names no place.** The word on it is the borrowed word and it is printed four times, and a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said so out loud before anybody asked and distinguished it from the gate at the end of that yard.
- **The finding of the block on the toll.** A toll is a thing a named person pays under a term. A party is not a person. A toll that cannot be fastened to a person cannot be collected and cannot be refused either, and the record on all four is that nobody was asked. **This yard decided it was the fourth of the four without being asked, and the man who says out loud that he is not going to be the man who decided it is the man who always says it first.**
- **TWO PLACES HOLD THE FOURTH OF THE FOUR.** This yard, because a sheet says so. A house at a road nine miles up, because a man of about forty-one walked down about four hours in the cold and said so on the fifth, and that his house did not sign and that the day they ask him is the day the house has signed without anybody going into the room. A clerk of nineteen years entered that two places hold the fourth and that neither can be shown to be right, and the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing refused the join of the two fours three times in three chapters.
- **A NOTICE, under the leg of the trestle table on the sixth of the fifth month, folded once and pressed into soft ground.** It names no place and no person. **It sets down a rival record: a book is kept in one room by one person, that person is the only one who may look in it, and that room stands four hundred yards from a yard a stranger can walk up to and walk in. A rival record is asked against. The body will ask it against four places and two of the four have not answered. The notice takes effect on the first day of the next month.** **READ *IDENTIFIED*, NOT *NAMED*. The notice identifies an office by what it does and where its books are. No name is written on it, no place is named on it and no person is named by it.**
- **THE ONLY LINE IN THE NOTICE THAT HAS ANYTHING TO DO WITH THE REGISTRAR IS HER OWN RULE**, and she wrote it on the twenty-first of the fourth month, before she knew there was a page in her office with a man's name on it or a chair on a table in that yard or a word printed four times on a piece of paper, and it is the only rule anybody in this district has ever written down and enforced, and it is also the only reason the notice could be written. **She said out loud that she cannot use this yard's rule about a name put on a figure, because nobody has put her name on anything, and that what is on the notice is her room and her books and the fact that she is the only one who may look in them, and all three of those are true and she wrote two of them herself, and that she cannot get a true thing off a notice by writing to a body four hundred miles away because she would be writing to tell them a thing they have already got right.** That is the block's political cost and it is not a comfort.
- **THE LOCK.** A frame of cast iron about four feet high, bolted to two pieces of oak let into the bank, with a screw through the middle of it, a handle about a foot and a half long, a pin through the screw where the handle is pinned on, and a stone sill about two feet wide with one notch cut in it. It weighs about a hundred and ninety pounds. It stands at the sluice end of the loams. It came out of its channel onto a barrow at about seven in the morning on the fourth of the fifth month and went up a bank in about eleven minutes with two men on the shafts and was put back the same afternoon. The barrow is not an instrument this district built and not named and did not become a seventh.
- **WHAT A LOCK HOLDS WITH.** **The pin takes nothing and holds nothing except the screw's own turning. The frame holds the channel and the weight of the water sitting on the frame holds the channel. The screw is a lever and not a wall, and a man who puts his weight on that lever is lifting the whole weight of a channel on one thread of iron.** The man of fifty-six is right and says so with his hand on the frame next to the other man's hand, out of nine years of putting a stick through that pin about four thousand times with both hands in the water. **A lock is the only instrument in this basin that has never needed a person, and it works in the middle of an ordinary night with nobody near it, and all nine of them will do it again tomorrow without asking any of us for anything.**
- **WHAT THE NOTCH IN THE SILL IS.** **One notch, cut for the edge of a plank. The board goes in it. The water is held above the board about a foot deep and no more. The board is not there because it is May, and it stands on end against the north wall of a building nine miles up a road.** The man of about forty-one who keeps that road house is right and says so with his thumb in the notch. **A notch is not a gauge, and a mark that says where a board goes is not a mark that says how high the water got, and a mark on a thing does not become a job because anybody decides to read it.**
- **THE LEDGER WENT BACK ON THE SEVENTH OF THE FIFTH MONTH, after fifteen days on a table four feet high under a hatch, on a morning when the man of about nineteen who keeps that counter was well, and nobody asked him and nobody was in the room. A person who has not been asked to carry a thing and has carried it has not refused it and has not consented to it, and the record about the fourteen days before that morning says not asked.**
- **The tank four miles down a lane is over its lip, the crack is about the length of a hand and is not going to close, there are two chalk lines on the inside of the brick, nine years of beds are in the second room, and the man of about fifty-five has not asked anybody for a mend in four months and refused again on the seventh and sent nothing on the tenth.** A crack in a tank is not a sixth protected thing. A man who drew two lines on the inside of a tank in the dark is not a clerk of a house and is not on a page.
- **The chair that has stood at the end of the trestle table since the twenty-third of April was still standing there on the twelfth of the fifth month, nineteen days, and nobody has sat in it and it has been moved four feet once.** A chair is a place a person is put and is not a bearer and is not a holder and is not a founder and did not become a founder by standing still.

## The two errors, and who was right

**Both are on the page and both were said out loud by the man who made them, in his own words, in front of witnesses, and a clerk entered each time that neither saying is a figure and neither is a column.**

1. **Chapter 232, a yard, about nineteen people, ninety-six words.** He said that what a lock holds with is the pin and that the pin is the piece that takes the load and that a lock is a safe thing exactly as long as a man is near it. **Wrong. The man of fifty-six is right, in a hundred and twelve words.** He then said the whole of what he was wrong about out loud, in a hundred and forty-seven words, and named the reason: a pin is not a wall, a screw is not a wall, a frame is not a wall, and a lock does not need a man to hold it and never did.
2. **Chapter 236, a bank four miles out past the loams, about nine people, ninety-one words.** He said that the notch in the sill is the record and that a mark like that is a gauge and a gauge wants somebody to read it. **Wrong. The man of about forty-one who keeps a road house is right, in eighty words.** He then said the whole of what he was wrong about out loud, in a hundred and sixteen words, and the sentence is **I read a mark as a job.**

**A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing proposed the whole behaviour as a seventh instrument in ninety-seven words and the clerk refused it in about four seconds, and the six did not move. On the eighth a clerk entered that the seventh has not been proposed a second time, and that a rule refused once and never again after that is a rule that has been learned and not a rule that has been dropped. On the twelfth the man of about thirty-four who digs loam said out loud that he was the one who proposed it and that a rule about being wrong is the same shape of rule as the sentence one of the nine wrote under NO PERSON SHALL HOLD THIS ALONE four hundred years ago, and a clerk entered that saying so is not withdrawing it and that the six are still six.**

## The instruments

- **A shut door and nine feet of lane are two different places and this block used both.** A room with a shut door in it is where a sentence goes to die and not a book; **about nine feet of lane is not a room with a shut door in it and is not a yard either, and a sentence said in about nine feet of lane in the last half hour of a day is not one of the three documents nobody owns and is not a fourth of them.**
- **A doorway is a fourth place and it is the only one this district has found this month.** On the eleventh a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing asked the woman of fifty-eight for leave, walked two hundred yards up behind the bank, lifted the hasp on a plank door that has not a handle on it, and put one question to one man of about forty-four sitting by his own door with a mended sleeve. **It was the first question anybody had put to that man in nine years, and he refused a toll with a reason and said he would answer a second question.** A man of fifty-six said that a man who has been asked one question in nine years and has answered it and has said he would answer a second is the only instrument this basin has that the four districts have not built yet, and that a yard which starts asking one question in one doorway is not a yard that is going to ask nine questions in nine doorways this week.
- **Two System panels in ten chapters, at Chapters 233 and 237, and not three.** Two further runs of `> **` are printed documents and are not panels: the toll sheet in Chapter 231, four paragraphs, and the notice in Chapter 234, four paragraphs. **Both panels were disagreed with out loud by a character in a yard, which is the second and third time in this volume that one person has done it twice.** No panel creates consent, makes a person a bearer, makes a house into a party or makes a no hold.

## The people, and what changed in the block

- **The man the figure of twenty-one years is against.** **Wrong twice about a lock, in front of witnesses, and said so out loud both times.** Said out loud in a yard on the eighth that he does not know what to do about four places and a notice and a pin, in eighty-nine words, and a man of fifty-six answered that a man who says that twice in a month is not a man without a plan. **In a room on the ninth he put both of his errors on her table himself, and in about nine feet of lane at about twenty to five on the eleventh he said out loud the fortnight of silence about a question he had asked her on the twenty-fourth of last month, and she said he did it on purpose, and he asked the other half of it.** On the twelfth he said he is going to ask the two places that have not answered, each of them separately, in their own doorway, and not ask one about the other. **He is not a bearer of a toll, is not a holder, is not a founder, is not a party of the fourth of four, and is not on the standing orders of the nine.**
- **The registrar of this district's records office.** **Identified by a document for the first time in this district, and identified without a name.** Asked in the six things in Chapter 234 and answered in a hundred and ten words. Refused a fifth condition with no end on it in eighty-four words. **Disagreed with a panel out loud in a yard in Chapter 237, in ninety words, that a notice affects a person before the day it is effective on.** Refused the man the figure of twenty-one years is against a copy of the notice going into a book, in a room with a shut door in it, and it is not in a book. **Walked four miles on the twelfth and put her thumb in the notch and said that somebody cut it four hundred years before her and that her name is not anywhere near it and that she would not write her rule a second time differently.** The last exchange between them in this block is three lines long and one of them is a man naming what he did not do.
- **The man of about thirty-four who digs loam.** **The body cost in this block is his and it is in scenes and not in a footer.** Carried the barrow up a bank in about eleven minutes at the near shaft on the fourth, said he was fine at the bottom of it and was not, and told the truth about that on the tenth, in a hundred and fifty-two words. Put his hand into a channel to feel the bottom of a sill on the eighth because a man he had met once asked him to. **Was not in the yard at all on the ninth and came back on the tenth with a sling the woman of fifty-eight lent him and nobody asked her to.** Disagreed with a panel out loud in a hundred and four words. Refused a fifth, sixth, seventh, eighth and ninth join in five chapters. Said the lock finding in eighty-nine words, the eight-of-eleven-nights finding in ninety-nine words, and that he could have walked to one of the nine in a morning and did not. **Said on the twelfth that a rule about being wrong is the same shape as the rule about being thanked that one of the nine wrote four hundred years ago, and that he was the one who proposed the seventh instrument.**
- **The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing.** Named the borrowed word before anybody asked. Refused the fifth column himself in ninety-five words. Proposed the seventh instrument in ninety-seven words and had it refused. **Walked two hundred yards on the eleventh and asked one man one question in his own doorway, after asking the woman of fifty-eight first, and wrote down what he said and did not argue with it.** Read the notice out in Chapters 234, 235, 238 and 240, five times in seven days in all.
- **The man of about fifty-six.** **Right about the pin, in a hundred and twelve words, with his hand on the frame.** In eight of the ten chapters. Gives the figures out loud, reads the board, and is not a clerk.
- **The man of about forty-one who keeps a road house at the top of the road.** **New speaking character and the one who is right about the notch, in eighty words, with his thumb in it.** Came down nine miles on the fifth, up again on the eighth. **He is not a keeper and has never been asked to be one, and a name over a door is a name on a house and not a name on a figure. He has no day in this district and no chapter may give him one.**
- **The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing.** **Refused two things with a reason and neither refusal moved the eight**: he would not copy the toll sheet, in eighty words, and he would not draw the lock, in sixty-five words. Made a fourth copy, of the notice, in about twenty minutes, for nothing. The district is still not going to pay him and has now said so five times.
- **The woman of fifty-eight.** Refused a toll on her two buckets in seventy-two words. Said yes in four seconds, with a reason, to a man going to ask a question at the ninth of her eleven houses. Said one thing about a sling, uncounted. **The twelfth bucket went up the bank and nobody counted the buckets. Nobody has priced a day.**
- **The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale.** Said in a hundred and nine words that the reason four houses are standing empty is that nobody has asked anybody in them a single question, and that the first thing anybody in this basin has to do is go and ask somebody. **Her four houses still stand and were not added to eleven.**
- **The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households.** Said in a hundred and six words that nobody had asked him anything that morning and that he was going to stop, and named the sixteen days the six have not moved. On the ninth said that a body asking four places a question is a body asking and not this yard, and that his six will know the difference because he has told them the difference.
- **The man of about fifty-five who keeps a building four miles down a lane.** Said in eighty-five words that the crack is not going to close and that he has not asked for a mend in four months and is not going to ask today. Sent nothing on the tenth.
- **The man of about forty-four at the ninth of the eleven houses.** **Asked one question in nine years on the eleventh and answered it**, refused a toll with a reason, and said he would answer a second question.
- **A man of about nineteen who counts.** Counted fifty-four sentences in these ten chapters and all fifty-four reproduce. Said on the ninth that it was the first morning since the second of April that he had counted nothing before about eleven.
- **The route keeper.** Walked through the yard on the tenth without stopping. On the eighth walked out to the top field with nine people, turned back at the field gate and went home, and nobody asked him where he was going. **He is not a bearer of a road and has never been asked to be one.**
- **The man with the portfolio.** **Still not in this district, off the page, and nothing in this block says where he is.**

## The counts, and the one that did not move against four attempts

**Things this district does not have five. Instruments built and not named six, and a seventh was proposed in this block for the first time in four months and refused, and two other candidates were refused on the page. Documents nobody owns three, and five documents arrived or were made in this block and none became a fourth. Protected things five and no sixth. Conditions with no end on it four, and the registrar refused a fifth herself in eighty-four words. Refusals to join two things thirteen and the registrar's own count twelve, and neither moved, and the man of about thirty-four who digs loam refused that join five times in five chapters. Refusals to read nine, the departure unspent, and no date in the ledger for the next reading. Boards five and lines full six, with the seventh line at the ninth place ruled and empty, and nobody put a line on a board on any of the forty-two days since the first of the fourth month. Columns of not-askings four, and a fifth was proposed in this block and refused in about four seconds, and a third candidate was refused in Chapter 239. A rate turning a year into coppers: none, and there never has been one.**

**THE POOL OF REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN IS EIGHT AND IT STOOD WHERE IT WAS ON EVERY ONE OF THE TEN DAYS AGAINST FOUR SEPARATE ATTEMPTS TO MOVE IT.** Four people refused something in this block, with a reason, and not one of them is a clerk of a house: **the man of about forty-one about a toll his house has not signed for, the woman of fifty-eight about her two buckets, the man of about forty-four at the ninth of the eleven houses about a toll nobody had ever asked him about, and the man of about twenty-three about copying the toll sheet and about drawing a lock.** **A refusal with a reason is not one of the refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given, and the clerk entered that at the point of each of the four.** The refusals about the ninth holding are seven and did not move. **The man of about thirty at a counter was not in this district on any of the ten days and no chapter gives him a day, and a clerk entered that on the eleventh. The next visit is the seventeenth.**

## The counting motif, fifty-four claims and no second-class claims

**This block carries fifty-four word-count claims in the first class and none in the second, and all fifty-four reproduce.** Class one: a number word attached by a claim phrase — *counted it and got*, *was counted and got*, *counted what he said and got*, *counted what she said and got*, *it came to*, *came to* — to a printed sentence standing in a paragraph that begins with a quotation mark, resolved forward to that paragraph, counted with words separated by spaces, a hyphenated numeral taken as one word, and the trailing full stop not counted. Per chapter, **seven in 231, six in 232, six in 233, six in 234, five in 235, five in 236, four in 237, five in 238, four in 239 and six in 240.** In order of appearance: 231 forty-eight, ninety-two, eighty-four, sixty-six, seventy-nine, seventy-two, eighty; 232 eighty, a hundred and twelve, a hundred and forty-seven, ninety-seven, sixty-five, eighty-three; 233 eighty-six, twenty-three, sixty-nine, seventy-nine, eighty-four, a hundred and four; 234 eighty-three, a hundred and ten, seventy-nine, eighty-four, a hundred and twenty-five, sixty-seven; 235 a hundred and thirteen, a hundred and six, eighty-five, eighty-nine, eighty-seven; 236 ninety-one, eighty, a hundred and three, a hundred and sixteen, a hundred and four; 237 sixty-eight, a hundred and seven, a hundred and nine, ninety; 238 a hundred and fifty-two, eighty-nine, ninety-nine, ninety-nine, eighty-nine; 239 ninety-six, seventy-two, forty-eight, eighty-seven; 240 a hundred and twenty-five, ninety-seven, a hundred and two, a hundred and four, eighty-eight, fourteen. **Class two, a span claimed in the form *in N words*: there is none in this block, and that is stated rather than presented as a virtue. A detector that sweeps only the first class cannot see the second, and the count of each class must be reported separately.** **Forty-eight of the fifty-four were written as a placeholder before the sentence under them existed, all forty-eight were wrong, and all forty-eight were corrected by recounting the printed sentence and not by shortening a single line of prose.** The detector used was calibrated on Chapters 221 to 230 first and finds forty-three class-one claims in that block against the forty-five its record enumerates, which is a limit of the phrase list and not a fault in the canon.

## Untouched in this block, to be left untouched

**The blank lot, Iven, the origin of the Common Measure, the First House, the removed signature, the three unexplained things of Volume 01, the office, the resolution of the romance, the name of the city on the agent's ledger, the name of the agent in any register in the west bank, the fourth line of the offer, the boy's name in a column in a building two days of road away, the two figures of five years and nine years, the iron, the echo of an origin toll, the Last Fare, the market stalls on the lower terrace, the first channel's failure and its sills, the four gaps, the two impressions, the two lines holding the office's name, the four lines of the broken circle, the signal's memory pattern, the pan that cannot report itself, the place that cannot report itself, and the mark on the iron. No place is named on any document in this district and none is named in narration. Cael Orin was not said a second or a third time. The ninth design signature belongs to Iven Tallow and Iven is not in the volume, does not appear, and is not named or foreshadowed. The nine local charters, the regional covenant, the letter calling this district's man a founder on nine walls and the Convener promotion are not in this block and are Chapters 241 onward. Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block. The slate is reserved for Chapters 241 to 250 and is untouched here.**

# Volume 05 Batch 0005 (Chapters 241 to 250) — the state the volume is written to

> **Additive to the section above, which is the position before this block. This is the last block of Volume 05. Chapters 241 to 250 are canon and the canon contract is `outline/batches/volume-05-batch-0005.md` and the block record is `state/volume-05-batch-0005-summary.md`. There is no Chapter 251 and no Batch 0006, and this phase wrote neither.**

## The world in this block

- **NINE LOCAL CHARTERS, EACH IN ITS OWN ROOM, EACH WITH ITS OWN BEARERS, ITS OWN TERM AND ITS OWN RIGHT TO GIVE THE WHOLE THING UP ON THE FIRST DAY OF ANY SEASON AFTER THE FIRST, AND NONE OF THEM ON ANY OTHER'S SHEET.** The first: five names under a line, each put there in its owner's own room with somebody in the door, a term of five seasons from the first of the next month. The second and third: three seasons and five seasons. The fourth: one season, with the reason given out loud by a man of about seventy-one, who said that a man of seventy-one does not get promised five seasons by a yard of about nineteen people on the strength of a good morning. The fifth and the sixth are in other places and the sixth sent four words back down the road on a piece of paper with nothing else on it. The seventh and the eighth this district has not seen. **The ninth came down the only road that climbs inside a coat, unasked for, with no names under the line, because the people at that place have not been asked.**
- **THE COST OF A RIGHT TO WITHDRAW IS ON THE PAGE AND WAS NOT ESTABLISHED BY ASKING THE PERSON WHO WOULD PAY IT.** It is in two speeches in Chapter 244, in a hundred and twenty-five words and a hundred and fifty-nine words, by a man of about thirty-four who digs loam who is not one of the five and does not live in any of their rooms. **A giving up does not cost money and does not cost a season and does not pull up anything sown. It costs about four hundred yards of drain and two other men's backs, and it costs a hole in the middle of the arrangement above four districts, and the people who are not in the five and have not been asked are inside the hole.** This district has no way to pay a person who is not in a household and has not priced the spending.
- **THE FIRST REGIONAL PUBLIC COVENANT IN THIS BASIN, ADOPTED IN A BARN ABOUT NINE MILES UP THE ONLY ROAD IN THIS DISTRICT THAT CLIMBS, BY SHOW OF HANDS, ON THE NINETEENTH OF THE FIFTH MONTH, AT ABOUT HALF PAST TEN, WITH ABOUT SEVEN HANDS UP AND TWO DOWN.** Read out twice in that barn, once by a man on a bin and once read back by a woman of fifty-eight. Copied by hand by a man of about twenty-three and read out at three of the four counters in this district; the fourth counter is a man of about nineteen who was not well enough to be asked to hold a sheet and was not asked. **Entered in the book in the room where the books are, and nowhere else, and a stranger may walk up to that door and read it there.** Four stewards changed every season by a show of hands; public tolls; a term of three seasons from the first of the next month; an appeal path by its own road into another region; no single holder; and the sentence **NO PERSON SHALL HOLD THIS ALONE** on the second line.
- **THE THIRD OF THE COVENANT'S THREE COLUMNS IS HEADED BY A WORD AND NOT BY A NAME AND IS RULED AND EMPTY ON THE FACE OF THE SHEET.** It is empty because the people who would go in it have not been asked. **The sheet itself forbids filling it and forbids going to a house that let a page be copied and asking it about a page it was allowed to copy in order to fill it.** A man of fifty-six said out loud that he was not going to say whether an empty line on a sheet from this month is the same shape of nothing as an empty place on a page out of a cave, and that anybody who wants to know can go and work it out and he was not going to work it out in a yard. A man of about twenty-three refused to keep a copy of the ninth sheet because a copy of an absence is a thing that travels.
- **A LETTER CAME INTO THE YARD OF LOT SEVENTEEN ON THE TWENTY-FIRST OF THE FIFTH MONTH, FOLDED ONCE, PUT THERE BY NOBODY, READ OUT AND READ BACK. IT NAMES A MAN TWICE AND NO PLACE, AND IT IS THE FIRST DOCUMENT IN THIS BASIN IN FOUR MONTHS THAT CARRIES A PERSON'S NAME ON IT.** It says the covenant has no founder, that he is the founder of the thing for saying it has one, that he may not take the name off and has said so out loud and has not asked anybody to take it off, **that the immediate vote is gone and that nobody in any of the four places can go on saying that a body four hundred miles away decides what happens to the water above them**, and that it is going on nine walls and that a wall is not an address and there is nowhere to send a reply to. **The Council acquired no face in this block. The man who read out a paper in the barn about the immediate vote was a man nobody sent for and a clerk entered that the record about him says not asked.**
- **THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST IS IN A COLUMN IN A BOOK A STRANGER MAY WALK UP TO, AND A NAME PUT ON A FIGURE CANNOT BE TAKEN OFF IT BY THE PERSON IT IS PUT ON, AND HE SAID THAT RULE OUT LOUD BEFORE HE TOOK THE COLUMN AND DID NOT ASK ANYBODY TO TAKE IT OFF.** A stranger stood at the gate of that yard at ten past seven on the twentieth of the fifth month, four miles on the strength of the name and not on the strength of a page, and asked whether the man who convenes was there, and was told he was standing about nine feet away with a bucket in his hand. **He is a Convener and the promotion is not a screen and the price is the last private thing he had.**
- **A LOCK WAS LOOKED AT FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS VOLUME, ON THE FIFTEENTH OF THE FIFTH MONTH, BY ABOUT NINE PEOPLE WHO WALKED NINE MILES AND ABOUT TWO HUNDRED YARDS OF SHOULDER PAST THE TOP OF THE ONLY ROAD IN THIS DISTRICT THAT CLIMBS.** The board was in the notch; the water was about a foot over the board; the road below was shut. **THIS DISTRICT CAN NOW SAY THAT, AND IT STILL CANNOT SAY WHETHER THE NINTH OF THE NINE NIGHTS HAPPENED ON THE TWENTIETH OF LAST MONTH, BECAUSE A PLANK DOES NOT KEEP NIGHTS.** Nine pieces of iron are finite and none of them is on any of the nine sheets and no person and no body is the holder of any of them.
- **THE REGISTRY'S NOTICE TAKES EFFECT ON THE FIRST DAY OF THE NEXT MONTH, WHICH IS TEN DAYS AFTER THE LAST DAY OF THIS BLOCK AND TEN DAYS AFTER THE LAST DAY OF THE VOLUME'S PROSE.** It was read out **seven times, the first thirteen days before the sixth and seventeen days before the seventh**, and on the twenty-first a registrar asked to read it in a yard of about nineteen people and got through the first line and stopped at the second, which is her own rule of the twenty-first of the fourth month, and the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing read the rest of it and nobody said anything to her about it. **A notice read out seven times in seventeen days in a yard a stranger can walk into is a notice this district has made its own twice over and has still not answered.**

## The instruments, and the one this block added

- **A door is still the only place a person can be asked a question and say no in their own words, and this block is built entirely out of doors.** A step of two stones and a plank door with a hasp and no handle; a second door about nine yards up the same lane with a handle and a curtain; five rooms in one house where a man of seventy-one put his own name under a line; a house on the far side of a valley where a sheet went under a door on the third of the fifth month and was never read out; a house about five people in it where the sheet was read out to the household; a kitchen doorway at the end of about four hundred yards of lane with a piece of chalk in it.
- **A slate is not a book and is not a page and is not one of the three documents nobody owns and did not become a fourth of them.** It has gone past that kitchen eleven times before and this was the twelfth, and no clerk in this district has written down on which days any of the eleven went past, and that was not repaired this month. **Two lines were written on it and they were about a fanlight over a door and not about the water, and the man who carried it back did not read them out in the yard, and the record about them is a minute in his own words and is not in a book.**
- **Three System panels' worth of printing appeared in this block and only two of them were panels.** Three printed documents, all introduced as a thing somebody reads out and read back because it is the rule of the counter: the first charter in Chapter 244, the covenant in Chapter 246, the letter in Chapter 249. Two panels, at Chapters 247 and 249, both arrived unasked and both disagreed with out loud by a character, once in a barn and once in a yard. **That is the first time in this volume that two panels were disagreed with in two different rooms.**
- **A new place in the block's vocabulary, and it is not a room: a barn with one window about nine inches square high up on the south wall, a floor of beaten earth swept that morning and already going brown at the middle, and a lantern on a shelf that was not lit.** Nobody was fetched to it. Eleven of the nineteen people in it came up the road and the other eight live in the country above the water and came because it is their barn.

## The people, and what changed in the block

- **The man the figure of twenty-one years is against.** **Put in a column and paid for it.** He said the price out loud in a barn in a hundred and forty-nine words before he took it, he said the rule about names on figures himself, he did not deny the word founder when the letter came, and he answered a stranger at a gate without being fetched. He went two days of road twice and asked each of the two places that had not answered in its own doorway and wrote down what he was told in their own words. **He is a Convener, is not a founder, is not a holder, is not a bearer of a toll, is not a party of the fourth of four, and is not on the standing orders of the nine.**
- **The registrar of this district's records office.** **Refused to sign the first charter and said why in a hundred and forty-one words. Signed one line out of six of the covenant and said out loud which one and why.** Put the covenant in her book and nowhere else and none of the nine charters in there. **On the twenty-first of the fifth month she could not finish reading a four-line notice about her own room in front of about nineteen people, and nobody said anything to her about it.** She is not a bearer, is not a holder, is not a steward and is not a party of anything.
- **The man of about thirty-four who digs loam.** Said the cost of a withdrawal in two speeches he was not asked for, refused three joins in three chapters, disagreed with a panel out loud in a barn, went up nine miles and about two hundred yards of shoulder, could not get his arm above his head at the top and it took him two goes, and ended the volume standing up to the knee in a channel with his hand on nothing. **He is the man who tied a wire knot in about four seconds for a man whose wrist he had not been told about.**
- **The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing.** Put the skin off the inside of his left wrist on a wire above a two-foot drop at about half past ten on the fifteenth and said nothing about it, wore his cuff buttoned over it for four days, could not get his hand closed on a fence post on the nineteenth, carried the letter up the road that climbs and put it into nine things that were not envelopes, carried the slate past a kitchen for the twelfth time and did not read out what was on it. **He is the one who said the whole method of the nine out loud in a yard in a hundred and forty words so that a clerk could write it down.**
- **The man of fifty-six.** **Disagreed with the second panel out loud in a yard in a hundred and twenty-six words and is right: a figure is a thing, a thing cannot say no, and a person can.** Said nine people wrote that sentence four hundred years ago to stop a man exactly like this one, and said out loud that a sheet in a yard is not a page in a cave. Gave the count of what the month had got in a hundred and thirty-seven words.
- **The man of about forty-one who keeps a road house at the top of the road.** **Said in a hundred and fifty-six words that he did not put the board in and his father did not, that somebody comes in March and goes down again, that he does not know what he is called and has not asked, and that the day he asks is the day there is a man at the top of that road instead of a house. He has no day in this district and none was given to him.**
- **The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing.** Refused two things with a reason and neither refusal moved the eight, asked for eleven coppers three times, was refused three times, and made two copies for nothing anyway. **The district is still not going to pay him and has now said so seven times.**
- **The woman of fifty-eight.** Read the covenant back in a barn after saying yes in four seconds. **A bucket of hers is about eleven pounds full and nobody in this basin has ever asked her and nobody counted the buckets on any of the ten days.** She did not ask for the sling back and did not say she wanted it.
- **The man of about fifty-five who keeps a building four miles down a lane.** Said no to being a steward before he was asked, in a hundred and twenty-nine words, and asked for it to be read back. **He sent nothing to that yard on the twenty-second and nobody sent anything to him, and the water is still about a hand's breadth over the lip and the crack is about the length of his hand and the nine years of beds are still in the second room.**
- **The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale.** Gave the yard nothing, said no before she was asked, and said she would read the record back at the counter herself. **THE SEX WAS WRONG IN TWENTY-NINE PASSAGES OF VOLUME 05, AT THE DESCRIPTOR AND AT EVERY PRONOUN CARRYING IT, AND THE REVIEW REPAIRED IT. Closed canon is a woman: she is a woman in Chapters 141, 146, 151, 164, 165, 173, 177, 184, 187, 195 and 199, and she is a woman in all fifty chapters of Volume 05. Her age is thirty-six throughout and has never been in dispute.** **Her four houses still stand and were not added to eleven.**
- **The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households.** Asked whether his six were one of the nine so that he would not have to lie about it later, and said he was glad of a thing that was not on the paper. **His six have not moved on any of the twenty-three, twenty-six, twenty-eight or twenty-nine days this block that printed the figure. The figure at Chapter 250 is thirty-one by the same derivation and is not printed in Chapter 250. Corrected by the Volume 05 close.**
- **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a road.** Carried a lamp nobody asked him to carry, waited at a field gate nobody asked him to wait at, brought a fifth sheet down the road that climbs inside his coat, and carried the letter up. **He is not a bearer of the road and has never been asked to be one.**
- **A man of about nineteen who counts.** Counted fifty-three sentences in these ten chapters and all fifty-three reproduce. **On the fifteenth, on a shoulder in the cold, he counted nine men going up and nine coming off and did not count the board, the water or the four generations, and said so out loud.**
- **The man with the portfolio.** **Still not in this district, off the page, and nothing in this block says where he is.**

## The counts, and the one that did not move against five attempts

**Things this district does not have five. Instruments built and not named six, and a seventh was NOT proposed in this block and four candidates were refused on the page. Documents nobody owns three, and five documents arrived or were made in this block and none became a fourth. Protected things five and no sixth. Conditions with no end on it four, and no fifth proposed and none refused. Refusals to join two things thirteen and the registrar's own count twelve, and neither moved, and the man of about thirty-four who digs loam refused that join three times in three chapters. Refusals to read nine, the departure unspent, no date in the ledger, and the fourth line of the offer was not read out. Boards five and lines full six, with the seventh line at the ninth place ruled and empty, and nobody put a line on a board on any of the fifty-two days since the first of the fourth month, counting the first day in. **THE FIFTY-TWO IS DERIVED AND IS NOT PRINTED IN CHAPTERS 241 TO 250; the last chapter in the volume that prints the figure is Chapter 240, at forty-two. Corrected by the Volume 05 close.** Columns of not-askings four, and no fifth was proposed in this block. A rate turning a year into coppers: none, and there never has been one, and it is now printed on two of the three documents of this block.**

**THE POOL OF REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN IS EIGHT AND IT STOOD WHERE IT WAS ON EVERY ONE OF THE TEN DAYS AGAINST FIVE SEPARATE ATTEMPTS TO MOVE IT.** Five people refused something in this block with a reason and not one of them is a clerk of a house: **the man of about twenty-three who copies for nothing, twice, the woman of fifty-eight, the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale, and the man of about fifty-five who keeps a building four miles down a lane.** The refusals about the ninth holding are seven and did not move. **The man of about thirty at a counter was not in this district on any of the ten days and no chapter gives him a day, and the next is the seventeenth.**

## The counting motif, fifty-three claims and no second-class claims

**This block carries fifty-three word-count claims in the first class and none in the second, and all fifty-three reproduce.** Per chapter, **eight in 241, seven in 242, four in 243, five in 244, seven in 245, four in 246, four in 247, five in 248, five in 249 and four in 250.** **The detector was calibrated on Chapters 221 to 240 before it was run on this block and returns forty-three and fifty-four class-one claims on those two blocks with zero mismatches, which are the figures their own records enumerate.** Seven of the thirty derived calendar fields were wrong in the first draft and all seven were corrected by counting; four further intervals were wrong and all four were corrected by counting; three counted sentences moved and all three were re-measured. **The block total is 28,057 words by `wc -w`.**

## Untouched in this block, to be left untouched

**The blank lot, Iven, the origin of the Common Measure, the First House, the removed signature, the three unexplained things of Volume 01, the office, the resolution of the romance, the name of the city on the agent's ledger, the name of the agent in any register in the west bank, the fourth line of the offer, the boy's name in a column in a building two days of road away, the two figures of five years and nine years, the iron, the echo of an origin toll, the Last Fare, the market stalls on the lower terrace, the first channel's failure and its sills, the four gaps, the two impressions, the two lines holding the office's name, the four lines of the broken circle, the signal's memory pattern, the pan that cannot report itself, the place that cannot report itself, and the mark on the iron. No place is named on any document in this district and none is named in narration, and the district's own name appears nowhere in Chapters 241 to 250. Cael Orin was not said a second or a third time. The ninth design signature belongs to a person who is not in the volume, does not appear, and is not named or foreshadowed. The ninth place on the page out of the cave is ruled and empty and was not filled. Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block. THE LOCK COUNCIL IS AN ANTAGONIST OF METHOD AND NOT A PERSON AND HAS ACQUIRED NO FACE IN THREE BLOCKS.**
