## The instruments, and the thing that was refused on the page

**A MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR WHO CARRIES A MESSAGE FROM ONE PLACE TO ANOTHER ON HIS OWN TWO FEET ARRIVED THROUGH THAT GATE ON THE TWENTY-FIRST OF THE SEVENTH MONTH WITH NOTHING IN HIS HANDS AND IS THE ONE NEW PERSON IN THIS BLOCK AND THE ONE NEW PERSON IN THREE HUNDRED AND TEN CHAPTERS.** No case, no portfolio, no list, no seal and no paper. He gave no name to anybody and nobody asked him for one, and the record about the not asking says not asked. **He is not a founder, not a holder, not a bearer, not a steward and not a party of anything, and a man who carries a message is not a keeper of it and does not become one.**

- **THE WORD FOR WHAT HE IS ARRIVED ORDINARY AND WAS CHECKED.** A man of about forty with a cart of his own off the lane said the word *courier* in that yard on the twenty-second of this month and meant a man who takes a parcel for a shop and is paid at the end of the week. **He said that was not what he was and said what he was in his own words without using the word, and a clerk entered that a word and a thing are two things and that a word somebody has been given is not a description of anybody. On the twenty-ninth he said the word for what he is and it is the same word and not the same thing.** `courier` is at six across the block, all of them in Chapters 302 and 309. **It is not a sixth of the protected things, not a seventh of the six instruments and not a fourth of the three documents nobody owns.**
- **THE ONE THING REFUSED ON THE PAGE IN THIS BLOCK, ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH, IS A PARCEL.** A man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart asked the man of about sixty-four whether he would carry a parcel up the eleven mile road to a turning about a mile short of a wall and take it back down again, and he refused in about four seconds with a reason of his own: a man who carries things gets asked to carry more things and he has not got room for the one he has got. **THE REFUSALS TO JOIN TWO THINGS ARE THIRTEEN AND THE REGISTRAR'S OWN COUNT IS TWELVE AND NEITHER MOVED, AND NO JOIN WAS PROPOSED IN THIS BLOCK. A REFUSAL WITH A REASON IS NOT ONE OF THE REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN, AND A MAN WHO DRIVES A CART IS NOT A CLERK OF A HOUSE.** The other two refusals with a reason in this block are the man of about thirty-four who keeps a road declining to say what eleven miles means on the twenty-sixth, and the man of about thirty-four who digs loam declining to tell the man of about thirty-seven that the table is his on the thirtieth.
- **NOTHING BECAME A SEVENTH OF THE SIX INSTRUMENTS THIS DISTRICT HAS BUILT AND NOT NAMED.** The eight things a Volume 06 clerk entered against the instruments are still entered against them, and this block entered nine more and did not add them to that eight: a man on a wall, about nine people under a frame of oak, a distance a man counted on his own two feet, a tent, a shape, a ladder of ten figures, a word, a name, a courier, and a figure of mornings. **A TENT IS NOT A SEVENTH, A HABIT IS NOT A SEVENTH, A PLACE IS NOT A SEVENTH, A FIGURE OF MORNINGS IS NOT A SEVENTH, A COPY IS NOT A SEVENTH, A BELL IS NOT A SEVENTH, A COLUMN IS NOT A SEVENTH AND A NAME IS NOT A SEVENTH. NO DOCUMENT ARRIVED OR WAS MADE IN THIS BLOCK AND NONE BECAME A FOURTH OF THE THREE.**

## The world in this block

**A yard a stranger can walk into with a trestle table, a chair nobody has sat in for ninety-eight days, a gate that takes two goes because the ground behind that post is soft, a board that has not been washed since the twenty-fourth of December, a hand-cart and a tent still rolled on the back of it against the wall inside the gate, a sheet of five lines of print on the end of the table with its corners gone soft, a piece of paper with two lines of print and the chalk mark of a third one started that has lain there since the fifth of this month, a low wall along the lane where a man of about sixty-four slept for nine nights and was offered nowhere, a pot of water put on at a gate about one by somebody, and two sheets on a gatepost, one of which has said the same number for fifty days and names a day that is eleven days off at Chapter 301 and two days off at Chapter 310.** A lane behind a bank with a wire on the left that has not been tight since before anybody in this district was born, a building with two doors about nine feet of brick and no window, a plank door in the end wall with a nail in a hasp that has not moved since the eleventh of the sixth month, a curtain on a nail, a table with four legs against the outside wall, and a ditch behind it about three feet deep with about a foot of standing water in it. A room about nine feet by eleven four hundred yards from that gate with a shut door, a handle, a step of two stones, a fanlight over the door that lights nothing, a shelf, two books, and one person who may write in them and was not asked anything on any of the ten days. A frame of oak about eleven feet high four miles out past the loams, two uprights about four feet apart, a bell about two feet across the mouth cracked from the rim up about a third of the way on the side that faces the loams, the clapper present, about nine inches of rope inside a beam where nobody standing on the ground can reach it, and a second frame about nine feet back holding nothing. A wall about four feet high with a gate in it that is not on a hinge, a turning off the eleven mile road about a mile before it that is not on any sheet, a field about two hundred yards across, and a ditch about three feet deep at the far end of it. A bank with a blanket of scale under a lean-to and a woman of about thirty-six at it.

## The people, and what changed in the block

**A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD WAS ASKED, FOR THE FIRST TIME IN FIVE VOLUMES, TO GO AND DO A THING, AND HE SAID THE REASON NOBODY EVER ASKED HIM IS THAT NOBODY ASKED HIM, AND HE WENT, AND IT COST HIM HIS LEGS.** He went out of that gate at about ten to five on the morning of the twenty-fourth, got out to the frame by about two in the afternoon, went outwards in daylight until the light went, started again at about ten to six on the twenty-fifth, went down into a ditch about three feet deep at the far end of a field about two hundred yards across at about eleven because he wanted to see what was past it, could not get up the first time, got up the second time about nine seconds later, walked back in from about half past eleven and came through that gate at about half past six in the morning of the twenty-sixth, and said the figure out loud in that yard at about half past ten. **He could not get back up the two goes of the bank into the lane on the first attempt on the twenty-eighth and got up on the second.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR CAME, STAYED, WAS FED BY PEOPLE WHO PUT THINGS DOWN NEAR HIM AND WERE NEVER ASKED FOR ANYTHING, AND WAS OFFERED NOBODY'S WALL, NOBODY'S LEAN-TO AND NOBODY'S FLOOR IN NINE NIGHTS, AND THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED.** He read the sheet at the gatepost and the five lines on the end of the trestle table from about four feet off on the day he arrived and came no nearer to either of them, and on the twenty-ninth he stood up off a wall in front of about nineteen people and said what he came for and stopped. **A NAME IS NOT A FIGURE, AND THE NAME WAS NOT SAID IN THAT YARD AND IS NOT IN THE ENTRY AND IS NOT ON ANY SHEET IN THIS DISTRICT.**

**The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale said the word EVEN out loud before anybody asked her for it on the twenty-sixth, and said that even is a word about a beam and that she has not had either of those two figures on her scale. It is not *rate* again and nobody asked her for it.** The man of about thirty-seven who puts tables up said the shape of the third thing he has not got twice in one day on the twenty-seventh, and the shape is a question, and a clerk entered that a shape noticed a second time is not a finding and a third time is a habit. The man of fifty-six worked three dates out loud on the twenty-second, said what a month is on the twenty-fourth, said the third of the three things a bell needs is not true and is not short by a rope, said nobody put one of the two figures there, and said on the twenty-ninth that a shape is worth nothing. The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing read the three things out loud on the twenty-fourth, said four or five words under the frame that the earliest day is not the day, and said three counted things across the block about not asking. **The man of about twenty-three who copies for nothing had his voice back on the morning of the twenty-first, as he said he would, and read the five lines out loud in about nine feet of lane outside the office to about four people on the thirtieth.** The man of about twenty-three's hand has not closed since the thirteenth of the sixth month and that did not change. The man of about thirty-four who digs loam was in the yard on the twenty-second, the twenty-fifth and the twenty-sixth, did not try to lift anything, and said on the thirtieth that his arm has not gone above the shoulder on any of the thirty days of the month and that he has still not told the man the table belongs to him.

Columns of not-askings four, and no fifth was ruled on any of the ten days. A rate turning a year into coppers: none, and **a month, a distance, a bucket and a bell are not one**. Readings of the rival record seven, its eighth reading refused out loud with a reason on the twenty-fourth of the sixth month, in force fifty to fifty-nine days, and **unanswered; this block does not answer it**.

A CLERK ENTERED THAT TWO FIGURES OF THE SAME LENGTH ARE TWO FIGURES AND ARE NOT ADDED TOGETHER, AND THAT A CLERK WHO COULD HAVE MADE A FINDING OUT OF THEM AND DID NOT IS NOT A CLERK WHO HAS FOUND NOTHING, AND THAT NOBODY PUT ONE OF THEM THERE. NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAID THE WORD COINCIDENCE AND THE WORD IS NOT IN THAT CHAPTER AND IS NOT IN THIS DISTRICT. THE ELEVEN MILES IS NOT STATED AS A COINCIDENCE AND NOT STATED AS A CAUSE IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BLOCK, AND NO MAN SAYS ANYTHING THAT WOULD MAKE IT EITHER. [A STRAY *A MAN OF THIRTY-FIVE* IN THE FIRST TEXT OF THIS SENTENCE WAS A FIGURE ABOUT NOBODY IN THIS CANON AND WAS CORRECTED BY THE REVIEW REPAIR OF VOLUME 07 BATCH 0001; THE THREE MEN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR IN THIS DISTRICT ARE THE FENCER, THE DIGGER AND THE ROAD MAN, AND A MAN OF THIRTY-FIVE IS NOT ONE OF THEM AND NEVER WAS.]** The difference between a number made by knocking and a number made by walking and a number made by listening is the volume's question and this block did not price it.

SIX WERE PROSE, FOUR WERE CLAIMS IN THE RECORD THAT DID NOT REPRODUCE AGAINST THE CHAPTERS THEY DESCRIBE, FOUR WERE THE WRITER'S TO JUDGE AND TWO OF THOSE WERE DISCLOSED RATHER THAN REPAIRED, AND FOUR WERE OUTSIDE THIS BLOCK. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO DATE MOVED, NO LADDER FIGURE MOVED — EVERY FIGURES SPEECH IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS IS BYTE-IDENTICAL TO WHAT IT WAS — AND THE ENDING OF THE BLOCK IS UNTOUCHED.**

**FIVE RELATIVE MONTHS WERE WRONG AND ARE FIXED, AND THE CLASS IS NAMED HERE BECAUSE IT IS THE ONE THAT KEEPS COMING BACK.** A man sat down on the end of that trestle table and not in the chair **on the sixteenth of the seventh month**, and four chapters said *the sixteenth of last month*, which is the sixteenth of the sixth, and `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0300.md` is the authority and says the sixteenth of the seventh. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a road said **on the eighteenth of the seventh month** that a habit and a figure are the same thing after a month and that he looked for a test for six days and did not find one — Volume 06's Chapter 298 is the eighteenth and has him saying it, and its Chapter 299 is the nineteenth and has him report that he said it on the eighteenth — and **the nineteenth of the seventh is the day the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing stopped in the middle of a sentence on purpose, six days after the man of about thirty-four who digs loam did the same thing on the thirteenth of the seventh.** The digger's statement that this district has never had a way of pricing a difference is **the twentieth of the seventh**, the day `chapter-0300.md` gives him for saying he has not told the man the table belongs to him. **THE THREE MEN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR ARE SEPARATED BY THEIR TRADE IN EVERY PARAGRAPH THEY APPEAR IN AND TWO OF THEM SHARE A NINETEENTH, AN EIGHTEENTH, A TWENTIETH AND A THIRTEENTH BETWEEN THEM. A READER WHO FINDS ONE OF THESE FIGURES IN A YARD MUST BE ABLE TO NAME WHICH MAN SAID IT.**

**AND TWO BODIES IN THIS DISTRICT ARE NOT TO BE SWAPPED. THE HAND THAT HAS NOT CLOSED SINCE THE ELEVENTH OF THE SIXTH IS THE FENCER'S LEFT HAND AND HE PUT A STRIP OF SKIN THE LENGTH OF A FOREARM DOWN THE SIDE OF A HEADSTOCK OF IRON ON THE TWENTY-THIRD OF THE SIXTH. THE HAND THAT HAS NOT CLOSED SINCE THE THIRTEENTH OF THE SIXTH IS THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-THREE WHO SELLS NOTHING AND COPIES FOR NOTHING. THE DIGGER'S IS AN ARM, AND IT HAS NOT GONE ABOVE THE LEVEL OF THAT SHOULDER SINCE THE FIRST OF THE SEVENTH. **THE YARD OF LOT SEVENTEEN IS THE ONE PLACE NAMED IN NARRATION IN THIS BLOCK AND IT IS NAMED IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS, TWELVE TIMES IN THE BODY AND SIX IN THE CLOSING LINES. NO OTHER PLACE IS NAMED: NOT ONE DOCUMENT, NOT THE HALL ELEVEN MILES OFF, NOT THE COUNTRY ABOVE THE WATER, AND NOT THIS DISTRICT'S OWN NAME. NO PERSON IS NAMED IN NARRATION.**

The woman of about thirty-one's eleven words are unspent and unapproached, she is in four chapters of this block, she is asked for nothing, and nothing in any of the four approaches, hints at, prepares for, rehearses or foreshadows them. ## The day, and what a day is worth when nothing happens on it

**CHAPTER 312 IS THE FIRST OF THE EIGHTH MONTH AND IT IS THE DAY THE SHEET AT THAT GATEPOST NAMES, AND ON THAT DAY NOTHING CAME.** There was no convoy and there was no fight and nobody was refused, because nothing was asked of anybody. The figure on that sheet was four hundred and eleven at ten in the morning and four hundred and eleven at half past eight in the evening. **A CLERK ENTERED THAT A DAY PASSING IS NOT AN ANSWER, THAT A SHEET WHICH SAYS *ON OR BEFORE* HAS NOT BEEN COMPLIED WITH BY A DAY PASSING AND HAS NOT BEEN UN-COMPLIED WITH BY IT EITHER, THAT THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT KNOW WHETHER IT HAS BEEN STOPPED OR IS LATE, AND THAT NOT KNOWING IS A FINDING AND IS ENTERED AS ONE.** The ladder that stood at one on the thirty-first of the seventh month has no term in it on that morning, and the day after a one is a day and not a figure.

**THE REMOVAL IS NOT SPENT AND THE BLOCK IS NOT A CHAPTER OF APPROACH.** The day is spent; the answer to it is not, and the answer is the next block's instrument.

## The instruments, and the thing that was refused on the page

**ONE NEW SHEET OF PRINT ARRIVED IN THIS BLOCK AND IT IS THE FIRST PRINTED MATTER IN VOLUME 07 THAT HAS COME INTO THAT YARD SINCE THE SEVENTH OF THE SEVENTH MONTH.** It was folded over the top of a stake in the yard's gatepost at about six in the morning of the seventh of the eighth month and came away with the stake when a man of about forty-five who digs and fills in holes for anybody who asks him took the top of the stake out of soft ground. It was read out once, in daylight, in that yard, at the request of the man of about thirty-four who digs loam, and the man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart read all five lines back from the top and got nothing wrong, **because that is the rule of the counter and a document is introduced as a thing somebody reads out and is read back afterwards.**

- **WHAT IT SAYS.** That the figure of the people in the ground in the shadow of a building eleven miles off this district is to be completed; that the figure is to be completed in the second book of the room four hundred yards from the gate and is not to be entered twice; **a word over a ruled line, and nothing on the line**; and that a figure that is to be completed is not a rate and the figure on a sheet at a gatepost is not to be entered again and is not to be asked for a second time.
- **WHAT IT NAMES AND WHAT IT DOES NOT.** No person, no body, no place, no signature, nothing at the bottom of it, and one building eleven miles off this district and nothing else. **A DOCUMENT WHICH NAMES A JOB THAT DOES NOT EXIST HAS FOUND NOBODY, AND THE JOB ON THAT SHEET IS *COMPLETED BY*, AND NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT IS COMPLETING ANYTHING. A COLUMN OF A THING WITH A WORD OVER IT IS NOT A COLUMN OF PEOPLE, AND NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT IS TO BE ENTERED IN THAT COLUMN AND DID NOT ASK TO BE.**
- **IT IS NOT A FOURTH OF THE THREE DOCUMENTS THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT OWN AND IT IS NOT A DOCUMENT THAT REGISTERS ONLY THOSE WHO CHOOSE TO ENTER, AND IT IS ENTERED ON THE PAGE AS NEITHER.**
- **ON THE EIGHTH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH A CLERK ENTERED THAT IT IS THE FIFTH OF THE SAME SHAPE OF THING IN THIS DISTRICT: the printed sheet nailed to a gatepost, the third column of a covenant in a book a stranger may walk up to, a line under a heading on the ninth of the nine charters in nine rooms, the ninth place on a page that came out of a cave, and this one. **NONE OF THE FIVE IS A PERSON AND THE FIVE ARE NOT ADDED TOGETHER, AND TWO OF THEM WERE RULED AND LEFT EMPTY IN THIS DISTRICT AND ARE STILL EMPTY AND NEITHER HAS BEEN FILLED ON ANY DAY SINCE THE TENTH OF THE SIXTH MONTH.****

**THE ONE THING REFUSED ON THE PAGE IN THIS BLOCK, AND THE OTHER ONE, AND NEITHER IS ONE OF THE THREE TALLIES.** A woman of about thirty-one who keeps a route for people who are not in any book refused the join of this yard to a charter in about four seconds with a reason of her own on the fifth of the eighth month, and refused a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing a second thing in about four seconds with a reason of her own on the sixth. **A REFUSAL WITH A REASON IS NOT ONE OF THE REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN, IS NOT A TENTH REFUSAL OF ELEVEN COPPERS A WEEK, AND IS NOT AN EIGHTH ABOUT THE NINTH HOLDING, AND THE NINE AND THE EIGHT AND THE SEVEN ARE THREE FIGURES AND ARE NOT ADDED TOGETHER. A WOMAN WHO KEEPS A ROUTE IS NOT A CLERK OF A HOUSE AND IS NOT A KEEPER OF A ROAD AND HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED TO BE A KEEPER OF ANYTHING.** No join was proposed in this block other than the one she refused. **NOTHING BECAME A SEVENTH OF THE SIX INSTRUMENTS THIS DISTRICT HAS BUILT AND NOT NAMED. THE THINGS ENTERED AGAINST THEM IN THIS BLOCK ARE A STAKE, A HOLE ABOUT A FOOT DEEP, TWENTY MINUTES, A LISTENING, A DISTANCE OF NINE FEET, A LINE WITH A WORD OVER IT, A COPIED LINE, A RUDELED LINE, AND A STEP OF TWO STONES.**

## The world in this block

**A yard a stranger can walk into with a trestle table, a chair nobody has sat in for a hundred and eight days which was moved about four inches and put back about four inches on the second of the eighth month, a gate that takes two goes because the ground behind that post is soft, a board that has not been washed since the twenty-fourth of December with a piece of chalk tied to one leg of it on a string about a foot long, a hand-cart and a tent still rolled on the back of it against the wall inside the gate, a sheet of five lines of print on the end of the table with its corners gone soft, a piece of paper with two lines of print and the chalk mark of a third one started, a low wall along the lane where a man of about sixty-four has slept on eighteen of the nineteen nights of his run, a gatepost with two sheets on it and a loose stake at the foot of it, and a sheet of print lying flat on the top step of two stones outside a room four hundred yards away.** A lane behind a bank with a wire on the left that has not been tight since before anybody in this district was born, a building with two doors about nine feet of brick and no window, a plank door in the end wall with a nail in a hasp that has not moved since the eleventh of the sixth month, a curtain on a nail, a table with four legs against the outside wall, and a ditch behind it about three feet deep with about a foot of standing water in it. A room about nine feet by eleven four hundred yards from that gate with a shut door, a handle, a step of two stones, a fanlight over the door that lights nothing, a shelf, two books, and one person who may write in them and was not asked anything on any of the ten days. An oak frame about eleven feet high four miles out past the loams with a bell about two feet across the mouth, a clapper present and about nine inches of rope inside a beam — **and the bell, the rope and the frame are not in any of the ten chapters of this block and the literal strings *a bell*, *the bell* and *a rope* are at zero across it.** A wall about four feet high eleven miles off with a gate in it that is not on a hinge, a turning that is not on any sheet, a field about two hundred yards across, and a ditch at the far end of it. A bank with a blanket of scale under a lean-to and a woman of about thirty-six at it.

## The people, and what changed in the block

**A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO KEEPS A ROUTE FOR PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT IN ANY BOOK CAME ON HER OWN LINE AND NOT ON ANYBODY'S, TWENTY-SEVEN DAYS AFTER THE LAST TIME ANYBODY IN THIS DISTRICT IS ON THE PAGE ABOUT HER, AND REFUSED TWO THINGS IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS EACH WITH A REASON OF HER OWN.** She came in by a gap in the wall about four feet wide and went round the ditch at the end of the bank rather than up the two goes in front of the yard. Her reason on the fifth is that nobody in that yard has asked her one question about her road in eleven years and nobody has asked one of the people on it anything either. Her reason on the sixth is that she does not keep anything in a room. **She is not the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a room with nothing over the door, not the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale, not the woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat and not the woman of fifty-eight, and a trade is the thing that tells them apart. SHE IS NOT A CONVERTER, NOT A FRIEND, NOT A PARTY OF ANYTHING AND NOT A KEEPER OF A ROAD. HER ELEVEN WORDS ARE UNSPENT AND WERE NOT APPROACHED, HINTED AT, PREPARED FOR, REHEARSED OR FORESHADOWED, AND SHE WAS NOT ASKED FOR THEM.**

**THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST ASKED HER ONE QUESTION ON THE FIFTH AND SAID OUT LOUD ON THE SIXTH THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO ASK HER A THIRD QUESTION, THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO ASK ANYBODY ELSE TO ASK HER ONE ON HIS BEHALF, THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO GO ROUND HER, AND THAT HE SENT NOBODY.** He also stood at a gate for forty minutes on the first of the eighth month and said in a yard that a day on which nobody asks anybody anything is not a day on which anybody has said no, and on the eighth of the eighth he said he has not knocked on that door in nine walks up that bank in a month and is not going to today, and that he has read a sheet that names that book out loud and has not handed it to her and is not going to and would like that entered as a thing a man did not do. **HE ASKED FOR NOTHING IN ANY OF THE TEN DAYS AND THE ROMANCE DID NOT MOVE.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR CARRIED A SHEET OF PRINT FOUR HUNDRED YARDS UP A BANK AND LAID IT ON A STEP AND DID NOT KNOCK, AND THAT IS THE COST HE PAID IN HIS BODY IN THIS BLOCK.** He took the two goes in about nine minutes, where a man of about thirty-four walks it in about four, stopped at the top of the first go for about a minute and a half and at the top of the second for about a minute, stood on the step with the sheet under his arm for about four minutes without putting a hand on the handle, crouched and put the sheet flat on the top step and squared it with the edge of a boot, and came back down in about eleven minutes. **HE DID NOT REPEAT WHAT HE SAID ON THE TWENTY-NINTH OF THE SEVENTH, NOBODY ASKED HIM TO, AND THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED. THE NAME IS NOT SAID AND IS NOT ON ANY SHEET.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING READ NOTHING OUT LOUD ON THE SECOND OF THE EIGHTH MONTH FOR THE FIRST TIME IN A MONTH** and said he had got nothing he had not read, and a clerk entered that a man not reading a sheet aloud in a yard on one morning is not a refusal to read and did not become a tenth of anything. He read the new sheet out twice on the day it arrived and twice on the day after, and **neither of those readings put a name near the ruled line.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP SAID THAT TWENTY-SEVEN MORNINGS IS A FIGURE ABOUT A TABLE AND NOT ABOUT A MAN WHO CANNOT SET IT UP ON HIS OWN, AND TRIED TO SLIDE FIVE LINES OF PRINT NINE INCHES ALONG A BOARD AND COULD NOT, AND DID IT WITH HIS RIGHT FOREARM.** The tent is still rolled on the back of that hand-cart and has not gone up on any day since the thirtieth of the sixth month. **The man of about thirty-four who digs loam carried a sack at the bank with his knees and his left hand on the thirty-first of the seventh month because his right arm will not go above his shoulder, could not get a sheet of paper off a trestle table with that arm on the second of the eighth, asked for the new sheet to be read out on the seventh, and said he was not going to put a name on a ruled line.** **HIS ARM HAS NOT GONE ABOVE THE LEVEL OF THAT SHOULDER SINCE THE FIRST OF THE SEVENTH AND HE HAS NO HAND THAT HAS NOT CLOSED.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD IS IN SIX OF THE TEN CHAPTERS AND PAYS NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK, AND THAT IS DISCLOSED.** His eleven miles and his ditch are Volume 06's cost and are not paid again.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE ASKED HOW MANY PEOPLE WERE IN THAT YARD, AND SAID HER FOUR EMPTY HOUSES ARE NOT GOING TO BE OFFERED AGAIN BEFORE THE FIRST DAY OF THE SEASON AFTER THIS ONE ON HER OWN STATEMENT, AND SAID THE WORD *STOCK* OUT LOUD, WHICH IS A THIRD WORD OUT OF HER AND IS NEITHER *RATE* NOR *EVEN* AND IS EXPLAINED BY NOBODY.** A man of about nineteen counted the yard and said nineteen, twice, four minutes apart, and a clerk entered that a head counted in a yard in daylight is a figure about a yard and that a head counted at a gate is a figure about a gate and that nineteen and about nine are two figures and do not make twenty-eight.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-FIVE WHO DIGS AND FILLS IN HOLES FOR ANYBODY WHO ASKS HIM IS THE ONE PERSON NEW TO THIS BLOCK** and is at the gate of that yard at the end of Chapter 311 asking whether anybody has lost a stake. He gave no name and was not asked for one.

**NOT ONE COUNT IN THIS BLOCK MOVED ON ANY OF ITS TEN DAYS, AND NOT ONE OF THEM WAS MADE LARGER.** Things this district does not have five, **and the phrase is at zero in all ten chapters and the five is not named as such in any of them, and the four empty houses that are named on the fourth of the eighth are a different four and not that five.** Instruments built and not named six, and **the entry is on two of the ten days, which is thinner than Batch 0001's eight and is disclosed.** Documents nobody owns three, **and one document arrived in this block and it is not a fourth of them.** Protected things five and no sixth, **and the entry in that exact phrasing is on two of the ten days and is carried in variants on the other eight.** Conditions with no end on it four, **entered on one of the ten days.** Refusals to join two things thirteen and the registrar's own count twelve, **and neither phrase is on the page in any of the ten chapters, which is disclosed, and no join was proposed.** Refusals to read nine, the departure unspent, no date in the ledger, and the fourth line of the offer not read out on any of the ten days, **and the entry is on one of the ten days.** Boards five and lines full six, a seventh ruled and empty at the ninth place, and **nobody put a line on a board on any of the hundred and twenty-second days to the thirty-first of the seventh and on any of the hundred and thirty-first days to the ninth of the eighth, counting the first of the fourth month in, and no line was put on one in this block.** Columns of not-askings four and no fifth ruled. A rate turning a year into coppers: none, **and the word *rate* is on the page twice, once in the new sheet's own fifth line and once in a clerk's entry that *stock* is not a rate, and a figure that is to be completed is not a rate.** Readings of the rival record seven, its eighth reading refused out loud with a reason on the twenty-fourth of the sixth month, in force sixty to sixty-nine days, **and unanswered; this block does not answer it and does not refuse it a second time either, and a figure of days about a record is not a reading of that record and did not become an eighth.**

**THE THREE FIGURES THIS BLOCK CARRIED AND KEPT APART: THE REFUSALS OF ELEVEN COPPERS A WEEK ARE NINE, THE REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN ARE EIGHT, AND THE REFUSALS ABOUT THE NINTH HOLDING ARE SEVEN. NONE OF THE THREE MOVED ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS, AND A WOMAN'S TWO REFUSALS ARE NEITHER A TENTH OF THE FIRST NOR A NINTH OF THE SECOND NOR AN EIGHTH OF THE THIRD. THE ENTRY THAT THE EIGHT DID NOT MOVE IS ON THE PAGE ON FOUR OF THE TEN DAYS — 311, 315, 318 AND 320 — AND ON NO PAIR OF CONSECUTIVE DAYS, WHICH IS BETTER ON THIS ITEM THAN BATCH 0001 WAS AND IS DISCLOSED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED. THE ENTRY THAT A REFUSAL WITH A REASON IS NOT ONE OF THE EIGHT IS ON ONE OF THE TEN DAYS, AT 316, WHICH IS A DAY THAT HELD A REFUSAL, AND CHAPTER 317 HELD A SECOND REFUSAL AND ITS ENTRY IS ABOUT THE TWO REFUSALS BEING TWO AND ABOUT NEITHER BEING ASKED FOR TWICE. NOTHING WAS CUT FROM ANY CHAPTER AFTER THE FACT TO MAKE THAT TRUE.**

**THE FIGURES THIS BLOCK ADDS TO ITS OWN VOCABULARY ARE THREE AND NONE OF THEM IS ABOUT A PERSON.** **THE FIGURE OF THE PEOPLE IN THAT YARD THIS MORNING, WHICH IS NINETEEN, AND WHICH A CLERK ENTERED AS A FIGURE ABOUT A YARD AND NOT ABOUT ANYBODY, AND WHICH IS NOT ADDED TO THE ABOUT NINE NAMES ON THE PIECE OF PAPER FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY AND DOES NOT MAKE TWENTY-EIGHT.** **THE FIGURE OF DAYS A RECORD HAS BEEN IN FORCE, WHICH IS SIXTY TO SIXTY-NINE, AND WHICH IS A FIGURE ABOUT A RECORD AND NOT A READING OF IT.** **AND THE FIGURE OF HOW MANY COLUMNS IN THIS DISTRICT HAVE A WORD OVER THEM AND NOTHING UNDER THEM, WHICH IS FIVE, AND NONE OF THE FIVE IS A PERSON AND THE FIVE ARE NOT ADDED TOGETHER.**

**EVERY RELATIVE-MONTH PHRASE IN THE BLOCK WAS READ AGAINST THE MONTH IT NAMES, SEPARATELY FROM CHECKING ANY LADDER, AND ALL SIX THAT ARE ON THE PAGE REPRODUCE.** From the seventh month *last month* is the sixth and *the month before last* is the fifth; from the first of the eighth month *last month* is the seventh and *the month before last* is the sixth; and the turn happened in the night of the thirty-first and Chapter 311 is the chapter that says so and a clerk wrote the whole rule down in one hand. **THE PHRASE *THE FIRST DAY OF THE MONTH AFTER NEXT* DOES NOT OCCUR IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS.**

**AND THE THIRD MONTH, WHICH IS THE THIRD FIGURE IN THIS PARAGRAPH AND THE ONE THAT KEEPS MOVING IN A MANUSCRIPT THAT PRINTS EVERY FIGURE. THE TWO FIGURES SIT IN THE RECORD UNRECONCILED AND NEITHER WAS SILENTLY CORRECTED.**

# Volume 07 Batch 0003 — Continuity, Chapters 321 to 330

**Additive to every section above. Volume 07 is open. Chapter 330 is the last day of this block. Nothing below was carried forward from another file and nothing in an earlier file was edited by this block.**

## The instruments this block made, and what they are not

1. **THE ROLL OF THE BUILDING WITH TWO DOORS**, eight lines on one sheet, drawn on the tenth of the eighth month in a room a stranger may walk up to, in one hand, and printed in full at Chapter 321, at Chapter 329 and at `outline/batches/volume-07-batch-0003.md` section 3.1, and all three copies reproduce character for character. **IT REGISTERS ONLY THE PEOPLE WHO CHOOSE TO ENTER, BY WRITING THEIR OWN NAME ON A LINE OF THEIR OWN, IN THE ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT, AND NOBODY MAY WRITE A NAME FOR ANOTHER PERSON. ITS COLUMN FOR EVERYBODY ELSE IS HEADED WITH THE WORD *NOT ASKED* AND HAS NOTHING UNDER IT, AND NOBODY IS TO BE PUT UNDER IT AND NOBODY IS TO BE ASKED WHETHER THEY WOULD LIKE TO BE. IT GIVES NO FIGURE OF ANYBODY. IT SAYS NOTHING ABOUT HOW A PERSON LEAVES IT, BECAUSE A PERSON LEAVES IT WHEN THEY GO.** It is not a fourth of the three documents this district does not own, it is not a seventh of the six instruments, it is not a sixth of the five protected things, and it is not a fifth column of not-askings. **A LINE WITH A NAME ON IT IS NOT A LINE WITH A WORD OVER IT AND NOTHING UNDER IT, AND THE CHARTER'S COLUMN IS THE SIXTH OF THE SIX SHAPES AND THE SIGNATURE LINE IS NOT A SEVENTH.**
2. **THE TWO TERMS**, on a second sheet, written on the thirteenth of the eighth month, the first in the right hand of the registrar of this district's records office and the second in his left, and printed in full at Chapter 324, at Chapter 329 and at the card's section 3.2, all three copies character for character. **THAT A PERSON MAY TAKE WATER, SHELTER AND MEDICINE IN THIS DISTRICT AND IS NOT ASKED FOR A PUBLIC IDENTITY IN RETURN; AND THAT NO PERSON IN THIS DISTRICT IS TO BE ENTERED IN ANY RECORD OF IT BY HAVING HEARD A SOUND AND NOBODY IS TO BE ASKED WHETHER THEY HEARD ONE. THEY ARE A TERM OF THE PUBLIC MARKET'S FOUNDING TERMS AND THEY LIE ON A TABLE WITH FOUR LEGS AGAINST THE OUTSIDE WALL OF THE BUILDING WITH TWO DOORS UNDER A STONE ABOUT THE SIZE OF A FIST. THEY ARE NOT IN THE BOOK FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY, AND THE REASON THEY ARE NOT IN IT IS THAT ONE PERSON MAY WRITE IN THAT BOOK AND SHE HAS NOT BEEN ASKED, AND THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED.** A clerk entered that **a term with nobody's name against it binds nobody, that this district found that out on the page and entered it, and that the term stands anyway**, and that a term nobody can enforce and everybody can read is a different thing from a term nobody can enforce and nobody can read, and that the standing of it is not a finding and is not a success.
3. **THE ELEVEN WORDS**, given once on the fourteenth of the eighth month at about four in the afternoon in a room about nine feet by nine at the top of that lane, past the third house from the scale, to the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing, chosen by her out of three people in this district she had reason to say it to. **THEY ARE NOT WRITTEN DOWN IN THIS DISTRICT, NOT IN A LEDGER, NOT IN A FOOTER, NOT IN A CARD AND NOT IN A BLOCK RECORD, AND THE ONLY FIGURE ON THE PAGE IS THE FIGURE ELEVEN, WHICH IS A FIGURE ABOUT ELEVEN WORDS. SHE DID NOT SAY THEM A SECOND TIME TO ANYBODY AND NOBODY ASKED HER TO.** They carry no name, no place, no figure and no body; they are a condition and not a promise; they do not say what she will do. **THE WORD *CLAUSE* IS USED ONCE IN THIS VOLUME AND IT IS IN A CLERK'S ENTRY AT CHAPTER 325 THAT SAYS A CLAUSE IS A DOCUMENT AND THAT ELEVEN WORDS IN A ROOM ARE NOT A DOCUMENT AND DID NOT BECOME ONE.** The eleven words were not approached, hinted at, prepared for, rehearsed or foreshadowed in Chapters 321 to 324, and she was not asked for them in any chapter of this block.
4. **A SIGNATURE IN CHALK** at the foot of that roll, in the left hand of the man who wrote it, small and pale and about a sixteenth of an inch off the line at one end. **THE NAME IS NOT PRINTED IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BLOCK AND NO CHAPTER ENTERS IT ANYWHERE. IT IS THE ONLY NAME ON ANY DOCUMENT THIS DISTRICT HAS PUT IN A ROOM A STRANGER MAY WALK UP TO SINCE THE CHAPTER THE SERIES BEGAN, AND IT IS THE SIGNER'S OWN NAME ON HIS OWN LINE, WHICH IS THE ONE PLACE THE RESERVED RULE PERMITS A NAME.**

**THE CHAIR AT THE END OF THE TRESTLE TABLE.** It had stood a hundred and nine days at the tenth of the eighth month and nobody had sat in it on any of them, and the figure is printed in that day's figures speech. **A MAN SAT IN IT AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST ELEVEN ON THE TENTH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH, IN THE BUILDING WITH TWO DOORS, TO WRITE THE ROLL, AND HE WAS NOT ASKED TO AND THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A MAN SITTING IN A CHAIR IS NOT A FINDING AND IS NOT A HABIT AND THAT A CHAIR IS A CHAIR AND IS NOT A DOCUMENT AND IS NOT A THRESHOLD AND IS NOT A SHELTER.** The chair was carried into the building in the morning and out again at about half past six and set down at the end of the trestle table about four inches from where it stood, and a clerk entered that a chair being carried is a chair being carried. **FROM CHAPTER 322 ONWARD NO CHAPTER SAYS *NOBODY HAS SAT IN IT* AND EVERY FIGURES SPEECH CARRIES *WAS SAT IN ONCE, ON THE TENTH OF THIS MONTH, AND HAD STOOD A HUNDRED AND NINE DAYS BEFORE THAT*, AND THE LEDGER OF CHAPTER 330 CARRIES THAT ONE SITTING IS NOT A FIGURE ABOUT ANYBODY.** The standing rule is that none of the counts that did not move may move without a reason on the page, in a scene, with a person in it. This one has all three and it is disclosed in the block record and here. **NO OTHER COUNT IN THE TABLE MOVED ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS.**

## Volume 07 Batch 0003 — the review-fix pass, and the four facts it corrected in canon

**A REVIEW OF THE BATCH 0003 WRITER COMMIT FOUND SEVEN FAULTS AND ALL SEVEN WERE REPAIRED WITHOUT A PLOT CHANGE, WITHOUT A NEW DOCUMENT, WITHOUT A NEW FIGURE AND WITHOUT A NEW SCENE. THE CHAPTERS REMAIN TEN CHAPTERS ON THE TENTH TO THE NINETEENTH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH. THE FULL REPAIR TABLE AND BOTH SETS OF MEASUREMENTS ARE IN `state/volume-07-batch-0003-summary.md` SECTION 10 AND IN `outline/batches/volume-07-batch-0003.md` SECTION 8. THESE ARE THE FOUR THINGS THAT ARE NOW CANON AND WERE NOT BEFORE.**

1. **THE REFUSALS GIVEN IN THE YARD OF LOT SEVENTEEN IN THE EIGHTH MONTH ARE THREE, NOT TWO.** The fifth, the sixth and the fourteenth. The fifth is at Chapter 316 and the sixth at Chapter 317 and the fourteenth at Chapter 325, and all three are refusals given in that yard by that woman of about thirty-one to a different man on a different day about a different thing. **The reed man of Chapter 322 IS NOT ONE OF THE THREE: he was refused at a table with four legs against the outside wall of the building with two doors at the near end of the lane behind the bank, and that is not the yard.** Chapters 326 and 328 were corrected from two to three. The eighth, the ninth and the seventh remain three separate figures and are still not added together, and none of the three refusals in that yard is a tenth of eleven coppers a week, a ninth with no reason, or an eighth about the ninth holding.
2. **THE RIVAL RECORD IS IN FORCE EIGHTY DAYS ON THE TWENTIETH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH, NOT NINETY.** Seventy on the tenth of the eighth and seventy-nine on the nineteenth, counted without the day it took effect. **The next phase's prompt printed ninety in its position paragraph and eighty in its figures bullet, and the ninety is corrected.** Ch 330 is 79, so the twentieth is 80 and the twenty-ninth is 89, and the block that opens on the twentieth is twenty-eight past the removal ladder at its last chapter.
3. **`state/volume-06-roll-summary.md` SECTION 1 LINE 50 DOES NOT PRINT ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-EIGHT AS A LIVE FIGURE.** Its live figures are sixty-nine at the first of the sixth and one hundred and seventeen, one hundred and eighteen and one hundred and nineteen in the seventh. **The one hundred and forty-eight is on that line only inside a bracketed note about a figure corrected on the twentieth of the seventh, which is a different month.** The canon card and the next prompt both mis-cited it and both are corrected. Chapter 330's one hundred and forty-eight is the nineteenth of the eighth and is derived from the months, not carried.
4. **THE PROSE OF BATCH 0003 HAS BEEN DE-TEMPLATED, AND THE MEASURED CONSEQUENCE IS RECORDED.** Ten identical chapter openings became ten different ones, ten identical closing-ledger openings became one, the byte-identical forty-word footer tail became a fourteen-word refrain, and an eight-chapter verbatim clause inside the figures speech became five variants. **The apparatus share fell from 44.8% to 42.3% and that is the honest figure, because the ledger is the canon and not slop: a figure that does not move must be entered every day it does not move or the next block cannot derive it.** Three new rules hold from Batch 0004 onward and are written into the next prompt's craft brief — a closing ledger may not repeat the previous chapter's opening construction, a ledger entry may not be repeated word for word inside one chapter, and the record-about-the-not-asking clause may appear at most twice in one chapter.

## The instruments this block made, and what they are not

1. **A KNOT IN ABOUT NINE INCHES OF ROPE**, tied on the twentieth of the eighth month by the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing, with the fingers of his right hand, because his left hand has not closed since the eleventh of the sixth month, and the loop of it goes over the inside of his right wrist. **THE REASON FOR THE KNOT IS IN THE BINDING AND NOT IN ANYBODY'S HEAD. A KNOT IN A ROPE IS A KNOT AND IS NOT A JOB AND IS NOT A DECISION, AND NOBODY DECIDED TO PULL THAT BELL IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BLOCK.** A man who tied one has not been asked to tie it and has not been asked to leave it. A bell does not need a person and a person standing on a frame is not standing in the bell's place.
2. **THE SECOND PULLING, AND THE RECORD OF IT.** The bell four miles out past the loams went at about two in the morning in the night of the twenty-first, for about nine minutes — about five of them in Chapter 332 and about four of them in Chapter 333, which stands on the twenty-second. **ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE WERE IN THE SOUND OF IT, AND NOBODY WAS ASKED WHETHER THEY HEARD IT, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS IN THE RECORD OF THE SECOND NIGHT AND CANNOT BE TAKEN OFF IT.** No door was shut. The building with two doors stood open on its hasp and the curtain on the nail hung clear of it and the two sheets of print lay on the table with four legs under a stone about the size of a fist. **THE THIRD OF THE THREE THINGS A CLERK WROTE DOWN ON THE SEVENTEENTH OF THE SIXTH MONTH WAS NOT TRUE AND WAS NOT MADE TRUE BY ANYBODY, AND THIS BLOCK REFERS TO THE THIRD OF THEM AND DOES NOT RESTATE THE THREE.**
3. **THE ACCOUNT OF THE NIGHT, AND THE LIST, READ OUT ON THE TWENTY-THIRD.** Two parts: the account of the night of the twenty-first, and a list of the people who were in the sound of that bell, called by the name each of them is called in this district, about nineteen long. Read out in daylight in about nine feet of yard in front of about nineteen people by the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing and read back from the top by the man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart, and nothing was wrong with either reading and neither reading was counted. **THE LIST IS NOT PRINTED IN ANY CHAPTER AND NO NAME IN IT IS PRINTED IN ANY CHAPTER, IT WAS NOT READ OUT AGAIN ON ANY DAY AFTER THE TWENTY-THIRD, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A LIST OF ABOUT NINETEEN NAMES READ OUT IN A YARD IS A LIST OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE WHO WERE IN A SOUND AND IS NOT A LIST OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE WHO CAME INTO A BUILDING, AND THAT A NAME READ OUT TWICE IS TWO NAMES, AND THAT EVERY PERSON NAMED IN IT IS NOW NAMED TWICE, ONCE BY A BELL AND ONCE IN A YARD.** It is not a sheet, not a document, and not a fourth of the three documents this district does not own.
4. **A COLUMN RULED IN THE PUBLIC BOOK FOUR HUNDRED YARDS FROM THAT GATE FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD**, a word over a ruled line with nothing under it, entered as the seventh of that shape of thing in this district, none of the six of them, not the same column as any of the six, not added to them, and not filled. **THE ONE NAME IN CHALK AT THE FOOT OF THE ROLL IS A LINE WITH A NAME ON IT AND IS NOT A SEVENTH OF ANYTHING, AND A MARK MAY NOT CARRY A JOB AND THAT MARK CARRIES NONE.** His name is not in the reading column and he is not going to ask the woman who keeps that book to put it there, and the one line in the second of the two books is not a substitute for it and was not offered as one.
5. **A NAME SAID ONCE IN A ROOM, AND ONCE IN A YARD.** See section 2 below.

## The name, and everything about it that is now fixed on the page

- **SAID ONCE, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, IN A ROOM ABOUT NINE FEET BY NINE AT THE TOP OF THAT LANE, PAST THE THIRD HOUSE FROM THE SCALE, ON THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH AT ABOUT TEN PAST FOUR, BY THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR, TO THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT, WHO WAS IN THAT ROOM FOR ABOUT TWENTY MINUTES.** There is no paper in that room and no ink in that room and there never has been, and a clerk of nineteen years was four hundred yards away in a yard and was not told about it until about six and did not go up that lane.
- **IT IS THE NAME OF A PERSON WHO IS ONE OF THE FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN, AND IT IS NOT NAMED WITH ANY PLACE AND NOT NAMED WITH ANY BODY, AND IT IS NOT ON ANY DOCUMENT IN THIS DISTRICT AND IS NOT ENTERED IN EITHER OF THE TWO BOOKS FOUR HUNDRED YARDS FROM THAT GATE. A CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS MAY ENTER THAT A NAME WAS SAID AND NOT WHAT IT WAS, AND THAT IS ALL A CLERK MAY ENTER.**
- **IT WAS SAID AGAIN IN A YARD ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH, BY THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT, COMING DOWN THE BANK AT ABOUT FIVE WITH TWO BUCKSETS THE WAY SHE DOES, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, BEFORE SHE ASKED ANYBODY FOR ANYTHING. THE TWO WORDS ARE NOT ON THE PAGE.**
- **HER REASON, IN HER OWN WORDS: A NAME THAT IS IN ONE HEAD IS A NAME THAT CAN BE GOT OUT OF THAT HEAD BY ANYTHING AT ALL, AND SHE IS NOT GOING TO BE THE ONE WHO HANDS A THING ON FOR A LIVING AND THEN BE SURPRISED WHEN SOMEBODY ASKS HER TO HAND IT ON AGAIN. HER OWN LINE IN THIS DISTRICT IS THAT SHE HAS ASKED THAT WOMAN ABOUT A BUCKET TWICE IN NINETEEN YEARS AND BOTH TIMES SHE ASKED HER ABOUT A BUCKET.**
- **THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED. A NAME SAID OUT LOAD IS A CONSENT THE PERSON IT BELONGS TO DID NOT GIVE, AND BINDING CANNOT CREATE CONSENT AND NEITHER CAN A CERTIFICATE AND NEITHER CAN A BELL. A NAME IS NOT A FIGURE, AND A NAME SAID OUT LOUD IN A YARD IS NOT ONE NAME ADDED TO THE FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN AND DOES NOT MAKE FOUR HUNDRED AND TWELVE, AND THE FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN IS A COUNT OF THE PEOPLE WHO ANSWERED A DOOR AND A NAME IN A YARD IS A COUNT OF NOTHING AT ALL.**
- **THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST WAS IN THAT YARD AND WAS THE ONLY MAN IN IT WHO KNEW WHAT THE TWO WORDS WERE, AND HE DID NOT SAY THEM BACK TO SHOW THAT HE HAD HEARD THEM, AND HE DID NOT GO UP THAT BANK THAT EVENING AND DID NOT ASK ANYBODY ELSE TO GO UP IT AND SENT NOBODY, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT HE ASKED FOR NOTHING.**

## The two costs, in bodies, and neither in a footer

1. **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR LOST THE EAR ON THE LEFT SIDE IN THE SOUND OF THAT BELL, AT ABOUT THE FOURTH MINUTE OF IT, AND HE DID NOT GET IT BACK, AND HE TOLD ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE IN THAT YARD ELEVEN MINUTES AFTER IT STOPPED, BECAUSE HE SAID ON THE TWENTY-NINTH OF THE SEVENTH MONTH THAT HE WOULD RATHER EVERYBODY KNEW A THING ABOUT HIS BODY ON A DAY HE NAMED THAN HAVE IT COME OUT OF A CONVERSATION ON SOME LATER AFTERNOON. THE FIGURE IS AN EAR AND IS A FIGURE ABOUT AN EAR.** He sat down on the low wall along the lane, which is where he had slept thirty-two of the thirty-three nights of his run. **THE NIGHT HE DID NOT SLEEP ON IT IS THE NIGHT OF THE TWENTY-FIRST AND HE WAS IN THE SOUND ON IT, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT HE WAS IN THIS DISTRICT THAT NIGHT AND DID NOT LIE DOWN, AND THAT THE NIGHT IS ONE MORE THAN THE STAY AND CLOSES ON IT ON EVERY ONE OF THE TEN DAYS.**
2. **THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST WAS ASLEEP IN A BED FOUR HUNDRED YARDS OFF IT.** He left that gate at about eight in the evening on the twenty-first, walked eleven miles up that road to a wall about four feet high eleven miles off where a table a man cannot set up has stood up fifty-two mornings, sat at it until the middle of the night, walked the eleven miles back in the dark on a right hand that has not closed properly since the tenth of the eighth month, was in a bed at about two, slept until about four, came out of the gate at about ten past four into a yard with about nineteen people in it who had been standing in it since about two in the morning, and asked nobody what had happened. **HE STOOD AT THE END OF THAT TRESTLE TABLE FOR ABOUT NINE MINUTES BEFORE HE COULD LOOK AT ANYTHING ON IT, BECAUSE THE YARD WAS TOO BRIGHT AFTER THE DARK. A SOUND THAT GOES ELEVEN MILES DID NOT WAKE A MAN FOUR HUNDRED YARDS OFF IT, AND THE ELEVEN MILES IS A FIGURE ABOUT A BELL AND THE FOUR HUNDRED YARDS IS A FIGURE ABOUT A MAN AND THE TWO ARE NOT ADDED TOGETHER, AND NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT HAS A WAY OF DRAWING A LINE, AND A CLERK WHO COULD HAVE MADE A FINDING OUT OF A SOUND AND A DISTANCE AND DID NOT IS NOT A CLERK WHO HAS FOUND NOTHING.** On the twenty-seventh his right hand closed about a stone about the size of a fist for the first time since the tenth, and a clerk entered that a hand is a figure about a hand and is not a figure about a document, and that the one book he would want to write in is the second of the two four hundred yards from that gate and a hand that can hold a stone is not a reason to go up that bank.

NO NAME IS PRINTED, NO NAME IS SAID, NOBODY IS ASKED FOR ONE, NO COLUMN IS RULED, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A MAN WITH NO NAME IN THIS DISTRICT IS NOT A PERSON ANYBODY HAS ASKED FOR ONE. ## 14. CHAPTERS 351 TO 360, THE STATE OF THE THINGS AT THE NINETEENTH OF THE NINTH MONTH, AND THE ONE THING THE NEXT BLOCK INHERITS THAT IT MAY NOT SPEND

> **ADDED BY THE BATCH 0002 WRITER AFTER THE PROSE. CHAPTERS 351 TO 360 ARE CANON. NO DATE, NO COUNT THAT DID NOT MOVE, NO LADDER FIGURE, NO AGE AND NO NAME CHANGED IN THE FILES BELOW, AND EVERY FIGURE IN THEM RE-DERIVES OUT OF THE NINE MONTH LENGTHS PLUS THE DAY IT NAMES.**

**1. THE READING OF THE LOT IS BEGUN AND NOT FINISHED, AND IT STOPPED ON THE SIXTEENTH OF THE NINTH AT THE THIRD OF THE FIVE THINGS A DOCUMENT THAT SETS A THING OUT HAS TO CARRY, AND HAS NOT GOT TO THE FOURTH OF THEM, WHICH IS A PERSON.** The fifth of the five, which is a remedy, has not been said. The lot was described a second time on the tenth with a figure attached, the term came off the reading on the tenth, the toll was established as unsayable on the eleventh because there is no holder, the inside of the lot was said out loud on the fourteenth by a man who was not asked to say it, and the man who convened the bid refused out loud on the sixteenth to say the fourth and the fifth of the five. **THE BID IS OPEN AND WAS NOT RUN ON ANY OF THE NINETEEN DAYS IT HAD STOOD AT THE LAST DAY OF THE BLOCK, AND NOBODY PROPOSED ANYTHING ABOUT CLOSING IT.**

**2. THE PANEL IS ON THE PAGE AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE IN VOLUME 08.** It is at Chapter 354. It is twenty-two words, it has no number in it, and it names a rule this district has been using in the ordinary voice for a hundred and forty days: a use ends when its term ends, and a person who held it may not hold it again without a new reading. It arrived in a room about nine feet by eleven, unasked, at about two in the afternoon, and the clerk of nineteen years, who was the only other person in the room, had her eyes on a page the whole time and had not seen it come. He argued with it out loud in that yard at about a quarter to four in front of about nineteen people and lost, and it did not answer him, and a clerk entered that a rule is not a document and that a man who has argued with one has not refused it and cannot be counted either way. **IT IS ENTERED ON THE PAGE AS NOT A SEVENTH OF THE SIX INSTRUMENTS THIS DISTRICT HAS BUILT AND NOT NAMED, WITH A PERSON IN THE ROOM. NO PANEL ARRIVED UNASKED AND DECIDED ANYTHING. ## 16. VOLUME 08 BATCH 0005 — CONTINUITY, CHAPTERS 381 TO 390, THE NINTH TO THE EIGHTEENTH OF THE TENTH MONTH

**1. THE POSITION, AND WHAT IS THE SAME UNDERNEATH IT.** Chapters 381 to 390 are the ninth to the eighteenth of the tenth month of the eighteenth year after the Long Fracture, one chapter a day, ten days and ten chapters, and Chapter 390 is the last chapter of Volume 08. The block opens on the day after Chapter 380 and closes on the day a man of fifty-six sat down in a chair. What did not move on any of the ten days: the figure on the sheet at that gatepost, which is four hundred and eleven; the rival record, in force one hundred and thirty days rising to one hundred and thirty-nine, answered once out loud on the fourth of the tenth and unanswered in the sense that matters; the bid, open thirty-nine days rising to forty-eight, not run on one of them, with nothing proposed about closing it; the reading of that lot, begun and not finished, at the fourth of the five three times and stopped three times, the fourth being a person and the fifth a remedy; the column for the name of whoever read a thing out loud, which is the seventh of that shape of thing in this district and was ruled and empty at about six on every one of the ten days; the second of the two books four hundred yards from that gate; the two sheets of print on the step of two stones; the ninth of the nine printed nights; the fifth of the five things this district does not have; the two refusals of a price for a name; the fourth line of the offer; the old charter's wrong fifth line; and the protagonist's name.

**2. WHAT CHANGED, IN TEN LINES.** A chair was sat in nine times on nine of the ten days. A rule about what makes a thing bind was said out loud in a yard in two hundred and two words with nobody's name in it, and its price was said out loud after it, and the price is that nobody can be made to keep it. The third of the three things this district has to have before it will call a sounding safe became true in that yard on the eleventh of the tenth, with a person in it and a cost in a person's asking. A woman of fifty-eight was asked, in daylight, in a yard, in front of about nineteen people, to read a thing back from the top, and read it back from the top, and got it right, and then said out loud that she is not going to do it again and that nobody has asked her to. A man of about nineteen was told in front of that yard that he is not the second reader and with a reason and was not asked for it. A right hand that had not closed properly since the tenth of the eighth month opened about four inches in the middle of a reading and a lot went under another man's forearm for about eleven minutes. The man who convened that reading is not the man who read it, and the toll cannot be paid because a building cannot be a holder and a building cannot say who is inside it. A second line was written in that lot book out of a figure a man gave with his mouth about a ditch, and a lot book gives a figure of a lot and not one figure of a person. A day's work was asked for in a yard by a woman of fifty-eight, done up to the waist, and not paid for. The bank was climbed, the door was not knocked on, one question was asked and answered once with a refusal and a reason of her own and not asked twice. Five different ninths in this district were written over five empty columns on one page in one morning and a sixth column of the same shape was refused out loud. Four accounts of one sentence were given to the man who said it and he corrected none of them. A body four hundred miles off did not arrive and acquired no face. A boy wrote nine at the top of his own page and took nine names off it in his own hand.

**3. THE RESOLUTION OF THE VOLUME, AND WHERE IT IS ON THE PAGE.** A thing in this district binds when a second person reads it back from the top and gets it right. It does not bind on a name, a signature, a mark or a person. A second reader is nobody in particular, and is a person in the yard who is not the first reader and is not the man who counts, because the man who counts is the one who catches the mistake and cannot also be the mistake. It is not a post, a column, a steward or a keeper, and there has never been a keeper on a page in this manuscript. It is found by whoever is standing there and is not found the same way twice. **THE PRICE OF IT IS THAT NOBODY CAN BE MADE TO KEEP IT, AND THE PRICE WAS COLLECTED ON THE THIRTEENTH OF THE TENTH, FOUR DAYS AFTER THE RULE WAS SAID, WHEN THE MAN WHO READ THE LOT OUT LOUD WAS NOT THE MAN WHO CONVENED IT AND THE ONLY PERSON WHO HAD READ A THING BACK FROM THE TOP SAID OUT LOUD ON THE ELEVENTH THAT SHE WAS NOT GOING TO DO IT AGAIN, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT THE RULE WAS NOT KEPT AND THAT NOBODY CAN BE MADE TO KEEP IT.** The rule is on the page, in a mouth, in a yard, in daylight, and it is not on any sheet, and no name went into any column, and the resolution is a rule and not a post.

**4. THE FIGURES, ALL DERIVED, NONE CARRIED.** Board `280 + f` = 289 to 298. Train `596 + f` = 605 to 614, and 324 + 281 = 605, 324 + 283 = 607, 324 + 290 = 614 reconcile the anchor and the base across the year boundary. Unentered `310 + f` = 319 to 328. From the second of January `271 + f` = 280 to 289. Pool of refusals `190 + f` = 199 to 208. The ninth of the nine printed nights `163 + f` = 172 to 181. The six households `162 + f` = 171 to 180. Days with no line on a board `183 + f` = 192 to 201. The rival record in force and the age of the figure about that figure, both `121 + f` counted off the first of the sixth with the day it took effect not counted, = 130 to 139, two figures and not one. Mornings that table has stood `86 + f` = 95 to 104. The removal day `60 + f` days past = 69 to 78. The stay of the man of about sixty-four `71 + f` = 80 to 89, and he slept on all but one of the nights. The bid `30 + f` = 39 to 48. The term on that reading `f + 1` days past = 10 to 19. The chairs: no figure of the age of either is on any page. The three alternative derivations of the board agree on all ten days and are printed. The trap of a count taken inside the eighteenth year alone was not fallen into. **AND THE TERM'S ANCHOR STILL DOES NOT ADD UP TO THE PAGE AND THE GAP IS NOT CLOSED, AND NO FIGURE IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS ADDS A MONTH TO A DAY OUT LOUD.**

**5. THE STANDING RULES THIS BLOCK PUT ON THE PAGE.** A person who has not been asked has not refused and cannot be counted either way, and the record says not asked, in three in-world forms. A document that names a job that does not exist has found nobody, and the word *keeper* is on the page four times and all four are a printed rule about a job, a noun somebody is told he is not, or a denial. A mark may not carry a job and a printed line may not either. A figure that reproduces is a figure about a count, and a yard getting four figures right and a boy getting the same figure four times is not a finding. A lot book gives a figure of a lot and not of a person who is in it, and is the tenth of what this district has made and not a seventh of the six. A toll is a thing a holder pays under a term, and a building is not a holder, and a building cannot say who is inside it, and a figure is not a person. A thing read back by nobody binds nobody. A yard is a room with no roof and is where a thing gets said out loud, and a thing said in one cannot be got back out of it. A chair is a chair and carries no job and nobody was asked to sit in it. A line in a lot book is a line about a lot and is not a line on a board, and a line that has stopped being true is not a wrong line and is not a mistake anybody made.

**6. WHAT WAS SPENT, AND WHAT WAS NOT.** Spent: the rule and its price; the third of the three things; a woman of fifty-eight asked to read a thing back from the top and doing it, and then refusing the next time out loud before anybody asked; a boy told he is not the second reader, with a reason, and not asked for it; a right hand that opened in the middle of a reading and a lot under a forearm; the reading read by the man who did not open the book; the toll and what a holder owes and that there is no holder; a second line in a lot book out of a figure about a ditch; a day's work asked for and done and not paid for; a bank climbed, a door not knocked on, one question and one refusal with a reason of her own; five ninths on one page and a sixth column refused; four accounts of one sentence and a man who would not correct any of them; a body four hundred miles off still not arriving and still with no face; and nine sittings in a chair a man put down without being asked. Not spent and not to be spent: the closing of the bid; any column filled and any name in any column and any person told they are a second reader; the two walls; the ninth of the nine printed nights, closed; the fourth line of the offer; the departure; the office; the second of the two books, touched; the eleven words; the name said out loud in Chapter 337; the protagonist's name; the romance, resolved; the man of about sixty-four, given anything, and the name of his second nine days; the bell, and any pulling of it, and any night; a length for the eleventh month; a second panel; a new person; a new place; a new faction; a new final enemy; a face on a body four hundred miles off; and the two past pullings and the name of the city on the agent's ledger and the name of the agent in any register in the west bank.

**7. THE TEN COSTS, AND NONE OF THEM IS IN A CLOSING LINE.** Legs that gave out in a wet ditch and a yard that said it was a chair, at 381. The blank side of a boy's own page, at 382. Two buckets in the mud and a standing woman's hands wiped on an apron in front of about nineteen people, at 383. A right hand that opened four inches in the middle of a sentence and a sheet that went into the wet, at 384. A left hand putting a book down the wrong way up and a clerk saying one word, at 385. Four minutes on a step out of breath and a question answered once, at 386. A man up to his waist in standing water who could not get the last two feet of a bank with his legs, at 387. A word missing out of a page and a clerk nine feet off who did not say one word, at 388. Four wrong accounts of one man's own sentence that he let stand, at 389. A boy's hand refusing to write nine names, at 390. **NONE OF THE TEN IS PAID IN THE SAME BODY AS THE ONE THE LAST BLOCK PAID, WHICH WAS A VOICE, AND NO CLOSING LINE IS THE LAST PLACE ANY OF THESE PEOPLE APPEARS, AND THE FINAL IMAGE OF THE VOLUME IS THE SECOND CHAIR WITH SOMEBODY IN IT AND IT IS NOT THE TABLE AND NOT A HAND AND NOT A SOUND AND NOT A SETTLEMENT AND NOT A HAND RAISED.**

**8. WHAT THE CLOSE INHERITS AS A DEBT, AND MAY NOT SPEND.** The debt is at `state/volume-08-batch-0005-summary.md` section 7 in full. In short: the reversal is resolved by a rule and its price is on the page; a lot book with two lines in it is on a table under a stone; a clerk's page with nine ruled columns and nothing in them is under the same stone; the column is ruled and empty; the reading is begun and not finished; the bid is open forty-eight days; the term is nineteen days past; the four hundred and eleven has not moved on any of the fifty days of the volume; the ninth of the nine printed nights is a hundred and eighty-one days back and was closed in none of the three days it was named on in this block; the old charter is still wrong on its face; the fifth of the five is still not paid; the interval a body four hundred miles off prints at is named once in this block and is not spent; and the man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart came into that yard once in this block and read nothing.

**9. AND THE STANDING DEFECTS, BOTH STILL OPEN.** The cross-chapter frame debt is 682 distinct twelve-word strings in more than one of the ten chapters, a fall of one hundred and fifty-nine on Batch 0004's 843 and the second fall in a row, and it is not closed, and a fall is not a clearance. The panel is still the only one in the whole of Volume 08 and is at Chapter 354, and the flag at `state/open-threads.md` section 13 item 1 stands, and a close does not fix it by putting a second one anywhere. **AND THE AGE RULE WAS APPLIED PER PASSAGE IN THIS BLOCK AND PER CHAPTER IN THE ONE BEFORE IT, AND BOTH ARE RECORDED, AND NO AGE WAS ALTERED IN EITHER AND NO MAN WAS RECAST AND NO NEW PERSON APPEARED IN EITHER.**

A man whose trade is over is not a failure and is not a tragic figure and is not redeemed, and a clerk may not say that about him and may not say the other thing either.

2. **THE SHORT FORMS THE PROSE NOW USES, BECAUSE A LATER WRITER WHO DOES NOT KNOW THEM WILL RE-INTRODUCE A FULL DESCRIPTOR ON EVERY MENTION AND THE REVIEW THAT REPAIRED THIS BLOCK NAMED THAT AS ONE OF THE THREE DISEASES.** **The mender** is the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing, and the card already used it. **The road keeper** is the man of about thirty-four who keeps a road. **The loam man**, and in the ledger voice *the man of about thirty-four who digs loam*, is the man who digs loam. **THE THREE MEN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR ARE THREE MEN AND THE TRADE IS THE ONLY THING THAT SEPARATES THEM, SO A SHORT FORM MUST CARRY THE TRADE AND *THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR* ALONE MAY NOT APPEAR.** **The boy** and **the man of about nineteen** are the same man. **The reed cutter** is the man of about thirty-seven who cuts reeds. **The man who puts tables up**, and *the table man* nowhere on the page, is the man of about thirty-seven who puts tables up. **The clerk** and **the clerk of nineteen years** are the same woman. **The man the figure of twenty-one years is against** is the protagonist and is not named, and **the man in the coat** is how the narration refers to him after his first mention in a chapter, and neither is a name and neither is a new descriptor of an age.
3. **WHAT THE REPAIR DID TO THE MEASUREMENTS, BOTH SETS PRINTED, AND THE FIGURE THAT IS NOW THE BASELINE FOR THE NEXT BLOCK.** Denominator 22,923; `wc -w` 23,049 with a per-chapter column of 2,563, 2,394, 2,211, 2,230, 2,286, 2,263, 2,414, 2,227, 2,182 and 2,279; 628 shared twelve-word runs; 20 class-one claims, 0 mismatches, 0 class-two; zero identical paragraphs of twelve words or more; 37 bold clauses; zero panels; an empty reserved scan; an empty integrity list; a clerk-entry share of 5.75 and 10.21 per cent; zero openings of the inherited shape; and the three standing strings, *the eleventh month*, *unchecked* and *eight months back*, all at zero, measured.

A LEDGER ENTRY IS ONE FRAME FOLLOWED BY SENTENCES A PERSON CAN READ. A YARD IS NOT A PLACE A NOUN CAN LIVE IN TWICE PER SENTENCE. A MAN SPEAKS AND ANOTHER MAN ANSWERS HIM, OR HE DOES NOT, AND IF NOBODY ANSWERS HIM THAT IS A THING THAT HAPPENED AND IS WRITTEN AS ONE. **THE COUNTING MOTIF IS THE SUBJECT AND THE SENTENCES AROUND IT ARE PROSE, AND A FIGURE THAT REPRODUCES IS NOT A CHAPTER THAT IS SOUND, WHICH THIS BLOCK'S OWN RECORD SAID BEFORE THE REVIEW SAID IT AGAIN.**

**4. THE STANDING RULES THIS BLOCK PUT ON THE PAGE OR KEPT ON IT, AND NO CHAPTER OF THE NEXT BLOCK MAY UNDO THEM.** A thing read back by nobody binds nobody, whatever it is about, and a reading of a figure and a word is not a reading of that lot. A figure anybody can put a finger on and a figure nobody can put a finger on are the same figure in a different place, and a district that works out which one it is living in has a job in front of it. A date a clerk names is not a lot, is not a figure of a lot, is not a figure of a person, is not a toll, is not a holder, is not a remedy and is not a term, and is not a seventh of the six instruments this district has built and not named, and it is not a fifth of anything. A figure somebody has entered because they had to enter one is not a promise about a later day, and the fourth time a person writes that sentence is not different from the third, and the third time is a habit and a habit is a finding. A figure entered off a list whose source nobody wrote down cannot be checked by anybody afterwards, and that is a cost about a board and not about a person. A word on a figure is a label and not a measurement, and a label is not a column, and a column with a word over it is not a column of people, and a new column of that shape is not an empty column and is not the same column and is not added to the old ones. A figure carried across a month nobody counted is a figure about a list and not a day-count, and a figure of a month said backwards in a yard changes on the first day of a month and the pages that carry the old figure are not altered. A rate is a thing that is already true of you whether anybody says it or not, and this district has not got one, and putting a figure on the length of a month is not how you get one. Nine days is a figure about how many days somebody has been in a yard saying a thing out loud and is not a figure about how many days a rule has stood. A rule that costs nothing is a rule nobody can be made to keep. A man who gets thanked for a thing stops knowing whether the thing is true. A document signed anyway is what a convening is. A man whose trade is over is not a failure and is not a tragic figure and is not redeemed, and a clerk is not going to say that about him and is not going to say the other thing either. A day somebody spent is not a thing this district has and is not a toll and is not a figure of money. A thing being clear is not the same as a thing being right. Nothing changing on purpose is a finding and not a habit, and one of the men who said so said he did not believe it.

Thirty was said out loud in that yard at about ten past nine on the fifteenth of the eleventh month, before anybody had read a figure off a wall. The man of about nineteen counted that at eighty-one. The man of about thirty-four who digs loam, who was in that ditch to his thigh at that moment, said back that he said a number in a yard and a man wrote it down and he did not give him a number to write down, and that he has been thinking about it for a month and has not got a better way of saying it.

**AND THE COST, IN A BODY. HIS RIGHT HAND CAME OUT OF HIS COAT AND WENT FLAT ON THE BOARDS OF THAT TABLE, PALM DOWN, BESIDE THE BOOK, FOR ABOUT FOUR MINUTES, AND WHEN HE TOOK IT AWAY IT DID NOT CLOSE ON ITSELF, AND IT HAS NOT CLOSED SINCE THE TENTH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH. THE MAN OF ABOUT NINETEEN WAS THE ONLY PERSON WHO SAW IT AND SAID NOTHING AND PUT NOTHING ON HIS OWN PAGE. ## 22. THE CONTINUITY EFFECTS OF THE REVIEW OF VOLUME 09 BATCH 0003, WHICH ARE FIVE SENTENCES IN TEN CHAPTERS AND ONE OPEN CONFLICT

**A REVIEW OF Chapters 411 TO 420 FOUND NINE THINGS. SIX WERE REPAIRED IN THE PROSE AND THREE ARE DISCLOSED AS NOT REPAIRED. NO CHARACTER CHANGED AGE, TRADE, HARM, STANDING OR KNOWLEDGE, NO NAME WAS PRINTED, NO COLUMN WAS FILLED, NO THREAD CLOSED, AND NO DATE MOVED. WHAT FOLLOWS IS ONLY WHAT A WRITER OR A CLOSE ACTUALLY NEEDS.**

**1. THE CROWD IN THAT YARD IS NO LONGER COUNTED THE SAME WAY, AND NOTHING WAS COUNTED THAT WAS NOT COUNTED BEFORE.** For ten days the block attributed almost every utterance to *about four people* or *about four of them*. After the repair it says *somebody said*, *a voice at the near end of that trestle table said*, *one said and another said*, *nobody said anything*, *everybody at that table looked up*, *three people watched*, *two of them*, and, in mouths, *half of you* and *the rest of you*. **The number of bodies is therefore stated less often and no figure is asserted that the chapters do not support. THE YARD STILL HOLDS ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE BY TEN EVERY DAY ON ALL TEN DAYS AND THAT FIGURE DID NOT MOVE, and the register the canon protects is *about four of somebody*, which survives at twelve.** A writer must not read the repair as a change in how many people are in that yard. It is a change in how often a speaker was counted.

**2. TWO SENTENCES THAT NAMED THE SPAN A CHAPTER BELONGS TO ARE GONE.** The close of Chapter 420 no longer says that the next ten days are the nineteenth to the twenty-eighth of this month, and no mouth in Chapter 413 says that a sentence is not said for the first time in the next ten days. **No person in that yard ever knew what span of days their day was part of, and now nothing on the page says otherwise.** The closing image of Chapter 420 is unchanged, which is the light going off the low wall and the shadow of it reaching the foot of the trestle table and stopping there, four feet short of the two lines.

**3. THE CLERK SAYS SOMETHING ELSE AT Chapter 420 AND IT IS STILL A REFUSAL.** She no longer says that a stay is a figure about a stay. She says that a stay is a count of nights in a district and that the man is not on it anywhere, and that the difference is the whole of it and she is not going to say it again this month. **The refusal, the price and the reason are the same three things they were. The sentence now substitutes rather than looping, and it still does not put a figure about a man in her mouth, which her canon forbids.**

**4. BOTH MEN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN CARRY THEIR TRADE IN EVERY MENTION FROM Chapter 412 ONWARD.** The man who puts tables up and the man of about thirty-seven who cuts reeds are still two men and the two trades still separate them everywhere else. **A short form without a trade — *the man who puts tables up*, *the man who cuts reeds* — is the form to use once the trade has been given in a chapter, and *the man of about thirty-seven* bare may not appear.**

**5. AND NOTHING ELSE MOVED.** The reversal at Chapter 415 is the same seventy-eight words a man of about nineteen counted and that reproduce. The thirty days said out loud at about ten past nine on the fifteenth of the eleventh is the same sentence. The book is still on the end of a trestle table with four legs, under a stone, in the open, on all ten days and on none of them taken off it. The man of about sixty-four is in his hundred and nineteenth day and has slept on a hundred and eighteen of the hundred and nineteen nights and folds his coat for a hundred and twentieth. The mender still has his left hand in its cloth, since the eleventh of the June, and still has said no five times and said nothing the sixth. The lot book still carries two lines and a figure on the second of them that is out of date, unaltered, with nothing written beside it, and *a foot and a half* is still at zero across the ten days. The column for the name of whoever read a thing out loud is still empty at about six on all ten days. Nobody went up that bank on any of the ten days. The tent is still rolled on the back of the hand-cart. The woman who keeps the two books is four hundred yards up the bank with her door shut. The romance is where it was: one visit on the fourteenth of the tenth, one question, one refusal with a reason, *thank you*, and no second asking.

### 22.1 THE ONE OPEN CONFLICT, WHICH IS A CONTINUITY PROBLEM AND NOT ONLY A CRAFT ONE

**THE WORD *UNCHECKED* IS MANDATORY ON THE TWELVE ARMS THAT CROSS THE MONTH BOUNDARY IN `outline/volume-09.md` SECTIONS 2.4 AND 10.1.2, AND CHAPTER 404 CARRIES IT, AND CHAPTERS 411 TO 420 DO NOT.** Across the ten days the word is at eleven and all eleven are in the margin of a clerk's page, and Chapters 414 and 418 say in their own sentences that it is over nothing and is not over any of the nine columns. **The arms do cross, from the tenth month into the eleventh, and the twelve are the board, the train, the unentered days, the days from the second of January, the pool, the ninth of the nine printed nights, the six households, the days with no line on a board, the rival record, the age of the figure about the figure, the mornings that table has stood, and the removal day.** `outline/volume-09.md` is the outline phase's file and was not edited. **A later block may carry the twelve arms with the word on them, which restores the outline, or may carry the inversion, in which case the volume close owes one sentence saying the volume's own finding came back empty. It may not leave the conflict unrecorded, which is what three blocks of records did.** No figure is wrong in either state of the world, and this licenses no other change.

### 22.2 THE FIGURES THAT WERE WRONG IN THE RECORD AND ARE NOW RIGHT, IN ONE PLACE

*About four* is **53**, not 111. *About four hundred* is **zero**, not 56, and has never once occurred. *About four people* is **8**, not 100. The ninth of the nine printed nights is on **three** days, 411, 414 and 417, not five. The days that carry *no bell was pulled and no night was named* are **412, 416 and 420**, not two. The identical-paragraph figure over Chapters 401 to 420 is **two**, both of them lines of the lot book, not one. *None at or over a hundred words anywhere* was false; a paragraph-boundary split gives a maximum of **128** and **two** at or over a hundred, the two remaining being Chapter 413 and Chapter 414, which are the two sentences the boy of about nineteen is made to count. `wc -w` is **25,901**, not 25,980. Shared twelve-word runs are **802** across the block and **1,871** over the twenty, not 805 and 1,884. The clerk-entry share is **6.43 per cent of paragraphs and 12.60 per cent of words**, the word figure being on the cap of 12.6 and not under it. **THE BLOCK COST 79 WORDS AND NO SCENE, AND ALL TEN CLASS-ONE CLAIMS STILL REPRODUCE AT 93, 81, 128, 120, 78, 87, 81, 86, 76 AND 81.**
